# ON METEOROLOGICAL MATTERS <note>original: *METEOROLOGICAE*</note>

...is, which may be heavenly in itself or hellish. Unless there were something at the boundaries and places where nothing human is or can be, in which Hell might be enclosed, and where those who have been cast out lie; or else they must be among us, without any power and joy, or banished and bound into the substance of the elements. For this is the greatest Hell: that they must exist and dwell within the perishable <note>original: *zergänglichen*; refers to the physical world which is subject to decay and time, in contrast to the eternal spiritual realm</note>, and not as they once were in their own kingdom. For such <margin>Hell.</margin> is surely to be considered a Hell: when an angel of Paradise is thrust out into the world and robbed of Paradise. No greater Hell could exist for a being that is eternal and has nothing perishable about it, than to be forced to dwell in the perishable; it can suffer no greater pain. 

Thus we conclude that the location of the four elements was formerly the heaven of Lucifer <note>original: *Luciferi*</note>, and he was not cast out of it; rather, that very heaven was made perishable and turned into the elements. This is his punishment: that he must remain within it. He had not expected that God would turn such a heaven and such a delight into elements; instead, he believed he would remain in heaven forever, and thus lose nothing. For when God created the four elements without his or anyone else’s say-so <note>original: *redt*; here meaning interference or consultation</note>, this world was created, and the creature was cast into Hell—that is, out of the light...

<summary>Paracelsus redefines Hell not as a separate location, but as the condition of being an eternal being (like Lucifer) trapped within the perishable four elements. He argues that our physical world is actually Lucifer's former heaven, transformed into a "perishable" state as a form of divine punishment.</summary>

Since the Air thus carries the other elements so that they remain in their places, it is for Philosophy to consider what lifts and carries the Air. The very same power that lifts it is the outermost part of the element wherein God dwells in the Trinity; He lifts the Air so that it does not give way, nor can it give way, nor can it break. For it is impossible that the perishable <note>original: *zergängkliche*; refers to the material world subject to time and decay.</note> should fall into the imperishable <note>the eternal realm of God.</note>; nor can it fall, because everything stands upward and nothing downward; it has no bottom or depth. Thus the Air is powerfully compacted and kept in a circle, so that it is as little possible for it to break or fray as it is for the outer kingdom to pass away. But if it should pass away or break, then there is no other breaking than that everything shall and will fall into the center—that is the Air—and the stars will fall into the globe <note>original: *globul*; refers here to the physical mass of the earth or the central point of the elemental world.</note>, and the globe will be consumed by them, so that no ash will remain there. ### Chapter Four <note>original: *Caput Quartum*.</note>

To speak now of the Element Air: know that the Air sustains all elements and creatures, so that they remain in their course, and persist in the Center <note>original: *Centro*</note>—that is, in the Circle and in the Ring. Thus the Earth and the Sea, being elements, are the middlemost of creatures, like the Center of the Circle from which the Element Air proceeds. **IMPRESSIONS.** <note>original: *IMPRESSIONES*, referring to celestial influences or the effects of the stars on the world</note>

It is a crude philosophy that theorizes that the sun draws [things] to itself, and thus whatever the sun draws up stands upright; much remains unanswered and forgotten there. This view has a poor reputation at the foundation of philosophy: for there is no such power of attraction in the **stars** <note>original: *Astris*</note> or the **firmament** <note>the vault of heaven</note> regarding their attachment. It makes us wonder how they intend to justify why the **stars** <note>original: *Astra*</note> do not pull plants up entirely; what is it that hinders them? But the spirit drives its fools.

### Chapter Five <note>original: *Caput Quintum*</note>

<margin>Motion of the heavens <note>original: *Coeli motus*</note></margin>

Thus, within the **element** <note>original: *Element*</note> there is a rising and a **ripening** <term>digestion<gloss>the internal process by which a substance matures and reaches its final state</gloss></term>. This process produces the vapor which provides us with the **Air** <note>original: *Lufft*</note> in the world, and it is a **clear fluid** <term>Clar<gloss>the subtle, life-sustaining part of the air, often compared by Paracelsus to the white of an egg</gloss></term> to support the **Center** <note>original: *Centrum*, referring to the Earth</note> of the world.

But so that you may distinguish between different **philosophies** <note>original: *Philosophay*</note>—namely, whether the heavens should revolve or stand still—the matter is this: if the heavens moved in a **circle** <note>original: *Cirkel*</note>, and if that **circuit** <note>original: *Cirkel*</note> were intended to create the air in the world, it would not happen. For if it were so, what would provide the **clear fluid** <note>original: *Clar*</note>? Upon what would the world and the sea then rest?

<margin>Wind <note>original: *Ventus*</note></margin>
Furthermore, such motion would only produce a **Wind** <note>original: *Windt*</note>, not Air. And yet, the fact that the wind remains still is a necessity and causes no harm. For when it remains still in its **expulsion** <note>original: *egestio*</note>, it provides the uniform Air that the world must have—neither more nor less—because within its **rotundity** <note>original: *rotunditet*; its spherical nature</note> it exerts a uniform influence upon the **Center** <note>original: *Centrum*</note>.

Thus, when a person moves from the upper part of the **Center** toward the sides—where there might be less air—he finds just as much air as at the **Center**, where air is most abundant. Note this well: the **Center** lies in the middle—that is, the world and the sea—and [it is said] the heavens should revolve around it. Therefore, note that this same wind, which is said to come from the heavens, and then...

# METEOROLOGICAL STUDIES <note>original: *METEOROLOGICAE*</note>

...always dryness is understood [with heat], and with cold, moisture is understood; and thus a matter is comprised of two virtues <note>or "qualities"</note>. Clumsy is the philosopher who speaks of four [elements], such as hot-dry, hot-moist, and so on, as if four elements existed; this is not so, for there are only two <note>The author is likely referring to the Paracelsian "Two Globes" theory, where Fire/Air and Earth/Water are paired as two distinct realms of existence</note>. Wetness and heat do not belong together; likewise, cold and dryness do not. It is like the comparison of a woman and a man, who are two, yet only one human,
<margin>The whole human <note>original: *Homo integer*</note></margin>
neither alone is a human, but they both together make the whole human and remain in one humanity. Thus, it is to be known regarding the elements that fire and air are one, and earth and water are also one.

Although it is the case that we may philosophize about the constitution <term>Complexion</term> <note>in early science, "complexion" referred to the specific balance of the four qualities—hot, cold, moist, and dry—within a body</note> of the elements, it will not be necessary for further satisfaction. For we may philosophize that fire and air have no shared constitution, nor do earth and water; rather, the heat from the fire is a fruit of the fire by which creatures live, and the cold of the air is also such, and the [dryness] of the earth is also a fruit for the benefit of the creatures. Thus, what the fire has dried out throughout the summer is moistened again by the winter for future fertility. And no other philosophy is useful except the philosophy of cold, as is indicated regarding cold in its own chapter. Although more and less cold and heat are found...

# METEOROLOGICAL MATTERS <note>original: *METEOROLOGICAE*</note>

And finally, this is the true **Philosophy** / that the elements exist in heat and cold / and not in moisture and dryness. For no hot thing is moist / and no cold thing is dry in its **Element**: dryness is in the **heavens** <note>original: *cœlo*</note> / just as vision is in the eye / and moisture is in the water likewise / vision does not make the eye / the eye does not make vision. Thus every **Element** has its companion / through which it becomes whole / like an eye that is made perfect through vision: So heat is made perfect through dryness / and so long as it has moisture / so long is no heat present there: just as little as an eye is without sight / and just as little as one may hear through the eye / or see through the ears / just as little is it possible / for cold to be dry / and heat to be wet. In this, the **Ancients of Philosophy** have erred / they set heat and moisture together in one **Element** which cannot be an **Element** at all / indeed, a **worm** <note>original: *Vermis*</note> may grow / which is warm and dry / but that is a composite <note>original: *ssnyhi*; likely a rendering of a term for an accidental or non-essential mixture</note> and not truly elemental: but some of the **Philosophers** babble / calling the **Substantial Complexion** <term>Elementum</term> <note>The "substantial complexion" refers to the specific mixture of qualities that the ancients believed defined a substance's nature</note> / and yet nothing there is a true **Element** / for it is the greatest lie / in both the **Ancient** and in the **New Philosophy**.

--- Page 78 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general philosophical discussion of the elements and their qualities to a specific introduction to the science of astronomy. Paracelsus begins to define astronomy not merely as the study of planetary movements (astrology), but as a broader discipline with multiple branches that have been neglected by his predecessors.</meta>

Preface.

# DR. THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSI
## ON THE SCIENCE OF ASTRO-
### NOMY.

<margin>A defense of the branches of Astronomy. <note>original Latin: *Astronomia Specierum vindicatio*</note></margin>
If astronomy is to be spoken of thoroughly—regarding what kind of art it is, or what is contained within it—it is necessary that it be described in all its **branches** <note>original: *Species*. In this context, Paracelsus refers to the various sub-disciplines or categories that make up a larger science.</note>, for it contains within itself branches from which anyone can and may wonderfully find things past, present, and future. Now, it is no less true that up until this time, by all astronomers before me, these branches have been neglected, and only one has been taken to represent the whole of **Astronomy**, namely **Astrology**. This alone has been maintained, and they have even called it the whole of astronomy and the whole of **Mathematics** <note>In the 16th century, "Mathematics" was a broad term that encompassed geometry, arithmetic, music, and especially astronomy/astrology.</note>, thereby abandoning the other branches. And they have not only been abandoned, but rejected, despised, and nullified, whereby it has come to pass that such arts have fallen into oblivion and have strayed from their true foundation—and yet they have ever been the best and the primary ones. These were also the branches in which all the secrets of the stars were hidden and lay, and through which branches great and powerful **secrets** <term>Arcana</term> <note>From the Latin *arcanum*, meaning "hidden" or "secret." In Paracelsian thought, *Arcana* are the powerful, hidden virtues of nature that can be revealed through true science.</note> are revealed and opened. Now, however, it should be known that in the first branch—that is, in astrology, which [the whole]...

<summary>Paracelsus introduces his treatise on astronomy by critiquing contemporary scholars for reducing the entire science to mere astrology. He argues for a restoration of astronomy's forgotten branches, which he claims hold the true secrets (Arcana) of the past, present, and future.</summary>
<keywords>Theophrastus Paracelsus, astronomy, astrology, mathematics, branches of science, arcana, hidden secrets, history of science</keywords>

--- Page 79 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface where Paracelsus critiques contemporary astronomers for focusing solely on planetary movements. He begins to outline his proprietary structure for the "true" science of astronomy, which he divides into five distinct branches.</meta>

ASTRONOMICA.
h ij
<meta>catchword: Astro-</meta>

...they have considered **Astronomy** to be only this: that they should be astrologers and nothing more, seeking nothing further and thus stopping there. But the astronomer should go further; he should know that five **Stars** <term>Astra</term> <note>Paracelsus uses "Astra" not just for celestial bodies, but for the internal "stars" or spiritual forces within man and nature.</note> arise from five **Astrologies**, and he who understands all five is a true **Astronomer**. Thus, **Astronomy** contains five branches within itself, and each branch has its own particular **Astrology**. These are:

1.  **Astrology of the heavens** <note>original Latin: *Astrologia olympi*. "Olympus" here refers to the firmament or the physical sky.</note> or of the firmament.
2.  **Astrology of magic**.
3.  **Astrology of divinations**.
4.  **Astrology of necromancy** <note>original Latin: *Astrologia nigromantiæ*. In this context, Paracelsus refers to the study of the "sidereal body" or the lingering impressions of the deceased, rather than modern "black magic."</note>.
5.  **Astrology of nature**.

Now you know that there are five parts which make an **Astronomer**. Where these five are not all found together, **Astronomy** is not truly understood, but rather a **part is taken for the whole** <note>original Latin: *species pro toto*</note>.

<margin>Division of the book. <note>original Latin: *Libri partitio*</note></margin>
Now, my intention is to demonstrate the order of the whole of **Astronomy** as it is comprised of these five branches. I will show what they are, what distinguishes each from the others, how they relate to one another, and how they are to be found together in a single **scheme** <note>original Latin: *figurę*</note>. Furthermore, I will explain what each branch accomplishes in its works and powers. This will be followed by a **Validation** <term>Probatio</term> <note>A formal proof or demonstration of truth.</note> to show that this science is righteous and natural; that it is not a deceitful or foolish art, and not contrary to **Nature**, as the ignorant claim and believe. Therefore, to explain these matters, the order, **definitions**, **effects**, and **validations** shall be described as follows. Within these things lies the first beginning, the first foundation, and the true understanding, as previously mentioned, without which no **Astronomy** exists or can ever exist.

<summary>Paracelsus defines the "true" Astronomer as one who masters five distinct branches of the science—celestial, magical, divinatory, necromantic, and natural—rather than just planetary observation. He outlines his plan to prove these branches are natural and legitimate sciences against the claims of critics.</summary>

->THEOLOGICAL KNOWLEDGE<-
<note>original Latin: THEO. SCIENTIA</note>

### Astronomy in general has four types.
<note>original Latin: Species</note>

**Natural** (Human and Ethereal) comes from the firmament; it was created to be one essential science <term>Scientia</term> and is assigned to the **microcosm** <term>Microcosm</term> <note>In Paracelsian thought, the "microcosm" refers to the human being, who contains within themselves a reflection of the entire universe (the macrocosm).</note>, etc.

**Satanic** of the infernal beings <note>original Latin: Infernorum</note> arises from the natural astronomy of the firmament, but is used only by infernal spirits. They are **natural astronomers** who are able to reveal things most skillfully, either through themselves or through human beings.

**Superior** <note>original Latin: Supera</note> has its essential dwelling with the celestial beings in heaven. It is given to those who are in the **New Birth** <note>A spiritual reference to those who have undergone a Christian regeneration or transformation.</note>; it takes its origin from **Christ** and is carried out through his own people.

**Of the New Olympus**, this possesses the power of the natural heavens and even more, and does so through **Faith**. It is given to the faithful and is completed through them.

<margin>Types of natural astronomy.</margin>

### Human Astronomy contains seven types or divisions, which are named as follows.

<margin>1.</margin> **Astrology** comprises three parts: the figure of the heavens <note>A reference to the horoscope or the arrangement of planets at a specific time.</note>, the properties of the stars, and the particular mode.

<margin>2.</margin> **Magic**, its types are five: Images, **Gamahey** <term>Gamahey</term> <note>These are stones or objects that naturally possess markings or signatures of the stars, believed to hold talismanic power.</note>, Likenesses, Words, and Herbs.

# ASTRONOMY.
Signature B iij
<meta>catchword: Ver-</meta>

3. **Divination** <note>original Latin: Diuinatio</note>, its four types: Dreams, Animals <note>original: Bruta; referring to omens observed in animal behavior.</note>, the Spirit, and Speculation.
4. **Necromancy** <note>original Latin: Nigromãtia; in this context, it often refers to communication with spirits or the "sidereal body" of the deceased rather than just "black magic."</note>, its four types: Scrying <note>original: Berelistica; specifically divination using a beryl stone or crystal ball.</note>, Fates, Visions, and Phantasms.
5. **The Marked** <note>original Latin: Signatum; the study of outward signs that reveal internal qualities.</note>, three types: Physiognomy <note>The study of facial features.</note>, Chiromancy <note>Palm reading.</note>, Bearing, and Proportion.
6. **Uncertain Arts**, four types: Geomancy <note>Divination by earth/soil.</note>, Hydromancy <note>Divination by water.</note>, Pyromancy <note>Divination by fire.</note>, Austromancy <note>Divination by wind/air.</note> (and Imagination).
7. **Manual Arts**, five types: Globes, Instruments, Geography, Cosmography, and Mathematics.

->Explanation of the Types.<-

**F**<image-desc>An ornamental woodcut initial 'F' depicting a stylized floral or landscape design within a square frame.</image-desc>igure of the Heavens <note>original Latin: Figura Cœli</note> is an art that teaches how the sky stands at every minute and makes the entire firmament known <note>This refers to the casting of a horoscope or astronomical chart.</note>.

**Properties** <note>original Latin: Proprietates</note> teaches the essence and characteristics of the stars and of all nature.

**The Particular Mode** <note>original Latin: Modus particularis</note> is to recognize the course of the heavens in relation to a single question; it is valid for the whole judgment and counsel <note>Likely referring to horary astrology, where a chart is cast to answer a specific query.</note>.

**Images** <note>original Latin: Imagines</note> are metallic or wax figures in which heavenly power works <note>These are talismans designed to capture planetary influences.</note>.

**Gamahé** <note>original: Gamahey; also known as cameo stones or naturally marked stones.</note> are stones imprinted with the celestial constellation and prepared through art.

**Likenesses** <note>original Latin: Similitudines</note> are heavenly figures that correspond to the things they affect.

--- Page 82 ---
<meta>This page provides a glossary of definitions for the sub-categories of the branches of "Human Astronomy" mentioned on the previous page. It defines the specific modes of operation for Magic, Divination, Necromancy, and the study of Signs.</meta>

### THEOLOGICAL KNOWLEDGE
<meta>catchword: Geo</meta>

*Words* <note>original Latin: Verba</note> or *Characters* are words that possess power when they are written or spoken.

*Herbs* <note>original Latin: Herbæ</note> are plants which possess celestial power beyond their natural <note>Meaning their physical or medicinal properties.</note> properties.

*Dreams* <note>original Latin: Somnia</note>; that is, an operation of the stars occurring during sleep.

*Animals* <note>original Latin: Bruta</note>; that is a power by which the [celestial] influence is revealed through beasts.

*The Mind* <note>original Latin: Animus</note>; these are celestial operations that occur within the spirit.

*Speculation* <note>original Latin: Speculatio</note>; that which the heavens work within a person while they are speculating <note>In this context, it refers to deep contemplation or theoretical inquiry.</note>.

*Scrying* <note>original Latin: Berilliſtica</note> is the art of crystals, fingernails, lenses, and mirrors, through which things are seen <note>The mention of fingernails refers to onychomancy, an ancient practice of gazing at polished fingernails to see the future.</note>.

*Fates* <note>original Latin: Fata</note>; chance occurrences and effects that happen beyond the common course of nature.

*Visions* <note>original Latin: Viſiones</note>; where spirits, apparitions, or *Specters* <note>original Latin: Spectra</note> are seen.

*Phantasms* <note>original Latin: Phantaſmata</note>; births born without humans, which are nonetheless regarded as humans and conduct their lives [as such] <note>This likely refers to Paracelsus's theories on elemental beings (like nymphs or gnomes) or homunculi, which he believed lived among humans despite different origins.</note>.

*Chiromancy* <note>original Latin: Chiromantia</note> is an art in which hands and feet, along with all other [features], are brought together for a judgment <note>A formal analysis or astrological reading.</note>.

*Physiognomy* <note>original Latin: Phyſionomia</note> is an art that examines the human face along with everything that is above the shoulders.

*Bearing* <note>original Latin: Habitus</note> is the art where the entire body is measured in its proportion and stature according to the art of *Geometry* <note>original Latin: Geometriæ</note>.

### ASTRONOMICAL MATTERS

**Geomancy** <term>Geomancy: A method of divination that interprets markings on the ground or the patterns formed by tossed handfuls of soil, rocks, or sand.</term>, an art performed through points without a natural basis; it depends upon the stars whether [the prediction] is brought forth or not.

**Pyromancy** <term>Pyromancy: The art of divination by means of fire, including the shapes of flames or the crackling of burning materials.</term> provides signs within fire; it is without foundation or certain indication.

**Hydromancy** <term>Hydromancy: Divination using water, such as observing ripples, colors, or reflections.</term> is an art performed through water; however, it possesses no more art than what a [mere trick] performs. <note>The original "gar" is likely a fragment of "Gauckler," meaning a juggler or illusionist, suggesting the author views this practice as mere sleight of hand.</note>

**Austromancy** <term>Austromancy: Divination by observing the winds (from the Latin 'auster', the south wind).</term> is an art of the winds; it succeeds only as far as the winds allow and is not a [reliable] art.

The reason the diagram of definitions was created and arranged with its categories <note>original Latin: "speciebus"</note> separated from one another is so that **Astronomy** might be understood more thoroughly. There are thus 20 parts <note>This refers to the total sum of the sub-categories of Magic, Divination, Necromancy, and Sign-interpretation discussed across these pages.</note> in which reside all powers, understanding, effects, and everything the stars are capable of; these are the mediums through which the stars act and through which their influence is brought into effect.

Now, I have first indicated the eight types of Stars <note>original Latin: "Astris"</note> that exist, but you should take note of this: like always seeks its medium within its like, and outside of this, nothing is accomplished. For example: **Piety** <note>original German: "Fromkeyt"</note> is carried out by the pious, and if something is to be stolen, a thief must do it. Stealing is not done by a pious person, nor is a pious deed done by a thief; thus, each thing remains and acts within its own kind. In the same way, the **Star** <note>original Latin: "Astrum"</note> in the firmament has dominion over all things, and within it are all the secrets of nature, and it is

The other four Stars are within the four elements, for the Earth also has its own Star, its course, motion, and order, just like the firmament, except that it is specifically directed toward the firmament. For there is an inclination <note>original Latin: "Inclinatio"; a natural tendency or celestial "leaning" that influences behavior or fate.</note> that cannot be turned aside; and through such operations of the upper and lower Stars <note>original Latin: "Astrorum"</note>, all things become fruitful and reach their completion.

The uppermost acts as the operation, and the lower four act as the medium. Thus, like works upon its like. This is **Man**, whom the stars have birthed; for the elements make up one part in Man—the animal body—the other...

...be much more mindful that God, for His work of Man, took a great composite <note>original Latin: *compositum*; a thing made up of several parts or elements</note>; namely, He gathered all His creatures together into one mass <note>original Latin: *massam*</note>, and out of that same mass He made Man. God created all other things with the Word <note>A reference to the *Logos* in Genesis, where God speaks the world into existence.</note> and made all creatures out of nothing—except for Man. Him He did not make out of nothing; instead, He made him out of a body that was called the "mass" or "dust." This is just as Christ also made the sick healthy with the Word, yet among others, He made a blind man see using His saliva. This could certainly have happened without the saliva, using only the Word; but it pleased Him to do it this way, so that the "mass" might be understood.

<margin>Microcosm from Microcosm</margin>
Therefore, because Man is made by God, God composed him out of all the simple elements <note>original Latin: *simplicibus*; the basic building blocks of nature</note> in heaven and on earth—out of everything that God created. From all of them, He extracted the quintessence <term>original German: *fünfft wesen*; literally the "fifth essence," thought by early scientists and alchemists to be the purest, most potent part of a substance that remains after the four physical elements (earth, air, fire, water) are removed</term>. Thus, Man is the lesser world <note>original Latin: *minor mundus*</note>, and the world in which Man [exists] is the greater world <note>original Latin: *maior mundus*</note>...

<margin>Why is man called the fifth essence?</margin>

### Particular Proof of the Science of Astrology <note>original: *Probatio particularis in Scientiam Astrologicam*</note>

<margin>The attractive force in man from the external sphere <note>original: *Vis attratrix in homine ex sphæra externa*</note>.</margin>Just as the birth of man has been shown, you should also know how man is sustained. Following this, Astrology is proven and understood in this way: you see that there is an innate power in man that draws from the outer spheres into the human being. From that which is drawn in, man is sustained, and by the same thing, he is made healthy or unhealthy, according to what he has drawn into himself. 

This is understood through this example: there are two attractive forces—one for the **elemental body** <term>original: *Elementischer leib*; the physical body made of the four elements of earth, water, air, and fire</term>, the other for the **sidereal body** <term>original: *Siderischen leib*; the "star-body" or astral spirit that connects man to the heavens</term>—and these are hunger and thirst. It is thus to be understood that the rays <note>original: *radij*</note> from the outer spheres extend all the way to us, and now into us; this must happen through us. Just as the Sphere of the Fruit gives its fruit even unto us, from the center of its roots to its outer regions <note>original: *locustis*; a Paracelsian term for the specific "places" or habitats where a substance exists or originates</note>. 

Now it follows further that we have an innate attractive nature within us, which is hunger and thirst; these compel us to eat these fruits. Now the rays <note>original: *radij*</note> of this sphere enter into us. This is also to be understood regarding bread; the Sphere of the Earth gives it to us, and through our attractive power, we bring it into ourselves. Similarly, with drink, we draw the same from the regions <note>original: *locustis*</note> of its sphere into...

### Proof in Magical Science.

<margin>What Magic is <note>original Latin: "Magica quid"</note></margin>
First of all things, I wish to give you instruction, starting with what **Magic** <term>Magica: here meaning the science of celestial forces, not stage tricks</term> is. Thus, through Man, the heavenly power may be brought into Man, so that in that same person there is found the very effect that is possible within that constellation. -># ASTRONOMICAL MATTERS<-
<note>original Latin: "ASTRONOMICA"</note>

<margin>How the **Magician** and the **Saint** differ. <note>original Latin: "Magus et Sāctus quo differant"</note></margin>
...[God has] also given out of Nature her power and ability to His **Saints**, and awakened them to do that which is not possible for others. Yet there is no difference between a **Saint** <note>original Latin: "Sanctum"</note> and a **Magician** <note>original Latin: "Magum"</note>, except only that the one works through God, and the other through Nature.

But it has long held me back from writing anything about **Astronomy**, and from speaking of it, whether with companions or otherwise, when I considered what power and force the heavenly influence has, and when I measure the coarseness and the misunderstood ways of men, who are not at all to be reasoned with. For the reason is this: where **Astronomy** with its **categories** <term>speciebus: the specific forms or types of celestial influence</term> is not recognized, who can recognize both lights? <note>Referring to the "Two Lights": the Light of Grace (divine revelation) and the Light of Nature (human reason and science).</note> Who can recognize both spirits? Who can even speak with such people, since everyone takes a side according to the influence of his own **stars** <term>Gestirn: the collective astrological influence or "constellation" of a person's birth</term>? And if someone now wishes to judge with certainty: many judge the **Magical** art <note>original Latin: "Magicam"</note> and speak of it out of a dry, rotten dream. Yet **Magic** is an excellent subject and great in its works. Since we know what **Christ** said—and His word is an abiding word—"if you believe, you shall do even greater things" <note>A reference to John 14:12</note>; if **Christ** performed certain deeds and yet promised the disciples they would do more, and if **Nature** performs something through her art, why should we not also do more than she? It has often happened that through such a skillful **Star** <note>The "Star" (Sidust) refers to a specific celestial influence or guiding force</note> and a well-born sidereal body, all future things have been prophesied that later came to pass. <margin>Treasures <note>original Latin: "Thesauri"</note></margin>

For the stars show all that is to come; nothing is hidden from them, whether in the past or the present. When such a skillful sidereal body is "found" by the stars—and the stars are ready—then wondrous things are revealed, regarding both the past and the present. Thus, women and old people have often prophesied, though they were gifted with no technical arts; as if in a state of folly and simplicity, they prophesied far into the future, which was later wonderfully confirmed. The reason is that the stars tremble at death; therefore, no individual dies without omens. ### ASTRONOMICA.

For as that same likeness within their constellation <note>original: "Gestirn"; refers to the celestial influence or star-pattern that governs their form.</note> allows, so many kinds of creatures appear that were not present in the first Creation <note>The initial creation of the world as described in the Book of Genesis.</note>; yet their raw matter <term>massa: the "dough" or primordial substance of life.</term> was indeed upon the earth, and through the alignment of stars <note>original: "Constellation"</note> it was spread out into that which was contained within it. Thus there were masses in the heavens <note>original: "Firmament"</note> which, through the influence of the stars, became **giants** <note>original: "Risengangen"</note>, some became **water-people**, some **mountain-mannikins**, and some **wood-spirits** <note>original: "Schretlen"; small forest or house spirits.</note>, and likewise passed away again along with that same constellation. 

<note>The following text from "Even though" to the end of the page is underlined in the original manuscript in reddish-brown ink.</note>

Even though it is true that we are all descended from Adam, and are those who should be called human beings, and God made our first father with His own hands without the influence of the stars—that is the creation of humanity—but there is yet one more thing for you to consider: that the children of Adam did not possess the entire world. For this reason, certain hidden lands were not settled by Adam’s children, but rather by another creature, also like humans, but created outside of Adam’s line of creation. For God did not wish to leave any place empty, and He wonderfully filled hidden lands with these other human-like beings, as is further explained in the treatise *On Generations* <note>original Latin: "de Generationibus"; likely referring to Paracelsus's work on the origins of life and elemental beings.</note>. We know well, however, that the four aforementioned types of human-like beings are predestined and appointed to the places and regions where they dwell solely through **heavenly influence** <note>original: "Himlischen Influenz"</note>, by means of a self-ordering matter <term>massa: the physical substance of their bodies.</term> and seed <term>sperm: here used in the alchemical sense of a life-spark or formative power rather than human biological seed.</term>.

<summary>Paracelsus distinguishes between the divine creation of Adam's descendants and the "sidereal" generation of elemental beings, arguing that God populated hidden lands with creatures born from celestial influence rather than human lineage.</summary>

As for how the exorcist deals with them, enough has been revealed to both parties.

But regarding the signs that Nature has presented, I wish to give further instruction: in the same way that you see openly when a seed is sown in the earth and hidden within it, Nature drives the secret of that seed up onto the earth, so that everyone sees what kind of seed was lying in that place.

Thus, every thing has its subject. # THEOLOGICAL KNOWLEDGE
<page-num>92</page-num>
<header>THEOLOGICAL KNOWLEDGE</header>
<meta>catchword: ha-</meta>

reveals what is within every single thing. He who does not possess such an **art**—to understand these three things and to recognize the secrets of Nature through them—can be no <term>naturalist</term> <note>original: "Naturalis"; here meaning a scholar of the natural world and its hidden properties.</note>, no natural <term>astronomer</term>, no natural physician, and no natural <term>philosopher</term>. He can know nothing of the hidden things of Nature, nor speak of her <term>mysteries</term> <note>original: "mysterijs"</note>. Thus we reach the fundamental principle: since all things possess a **seed**, and all things are contained within that seed, and Nature is the **maker** <note>original: "Fabricator"; the creative force that shapes physical matter.</note> of the form, she provides the form according to how the essence is in itself. This form reveals the essence, for the essence is invisible. Therefore, these three things belong to the study of the stars, so that in **conception** they are so ordered, created, and endowed. Thus their interpretation arises from astronomy, along with others born from this <term>horoscope</term> <note>original: "Horoscopo"; in this context, the specific "rising" or manifestation of a thing’s nature.</note>.

->### Conclusion.<-

<margin>
**Royal Arts** <note>original: "Artes Regales"; disciplines traditionally reserved for the elite or those with divine right.</note>
</margin>
Thus, I have seen fit to describe **astronomy** with all its branches, and to demonstrate them clearly with sufficient proof. It would be improper for it to be neglected, since it was held in such high and great esteem by the ancients, and was so privileged that no one was taught it except for kings and the **high priests**...

First cause:
Pride <note>original Latin: "Prima causa Superbia."</note>.

The first cause is the rooster, and it is well pre-figured; it indicates the exceedingly great and fierce pride of the French, as they carry and possess it through their <term>influence <note>original: "Influenz"; in astrology, this refers to the celestial power flowing from the stars down to Earth to affect human character.</note></term> over all nations. Just as the rooster is pre-figured—lifting his comb high, stretching his neck against the mightiest, and crowing with the greatest pride he can display—so too is this proud nature found in the French. They believe nothing else than that their necks, their reason, and their wits reach all the way into heaven. Whatever they do or negotiate, whether standing or walking, occurs with bustling gestures and such magnificent pride; just as when the rooster wakes and wishes to crow, he employs his pride in the most terrible way he can. Understand the French in the same way: their pride assumes they overlook the entire world, and when they stretch their necks, all the world should flee.

From this will then follow the indication of the **Constellation** <note>The astrological alignment of the stars</note>: that a Frenchman shall become the head of the **Holy Roman Empire**, in such a way that the Pope, in view of the King's good deeds, will assign the honor of the Empire—specifically the name of the **Eagle**—to him.

# PROGNOSTICATIONS

<margin>Pseudo-astronomers <note>original: "Pseudoastronomi"</note></margin>
...cess of the heavenly <term>Influence: the flow of power or energy from the stars to the earth</term>: and they never considered / that the light of nature / which namely shuns evil / and finds a horror in the vice of murder / etc. and thereby they have forgotten the great <term>Arcana: hidden spiritual secrets or powerful remedies</term> <note>original: "Arcana"</note>, which have driven out the heavenly <term>Influences</term> <note>original: "Influxiones"</note>, and further what is more / than all of those / namely the Faith in the heart / which is understood in the name of Wisdom / toward which we, through its greater strength / which has penetrated the Heavens / resist and overcome. ### Regarding Chapter XXI.

#### Here stands the Hungarian King in armor.

The praise bestowed upon King Matthias <note>Matthias Corvinus (reigned 1458–1490), King of Hungary and Croatia. He was a famed military leader and a great patron of Renaissance culture and astrology.</note> is indeed his due; however, what concern is this to the <term>astronomer: here used in the historical sense of one who studies both the stars and their influence on human affairs</term> <note>original: "Astronomum"</note>? The astronomer is moved only by the fact that the Heavens are bound to a man's good deeds just as much as to his malice, and that they govern within Man in a wondrous way. But what is this praise that he attributes to the Heavens? Such a man entrusts his life to the Heavens and his death to the stars. For he thus regards the body—which the Heavens fortunately gave him—as his God. Even though such a man performs many excellent and good deeds, anyone who could perceive the blemish of the soul within him would discover how [differently] the fortunate man who exists there would be if he were truly from God.

It is a great thing to consider whether we humans—who spend our time on Earth performing wondrous and useful feats and who serve the Fatherland excellently—reach our positions through God or through the Heavens. For what is the praise of "possessing the Heavens"? It is nothing; it is entirely of the body, and the soul is damned by such praise. See an example in this: what joy does it bring the soul, whose body is melan-

### CONCERNING THE GAULS <note>original: "DE GALLIS"</note>

If a **Constellation** <term>Constellation: the relative positions of stars believed to influence human affairs</term> departs, how is that? It is just like the year; now it snows, now it rains, it hails, it is hot, or it is too cold. The **Heavens** <note>the celestial sphere and its planetary movements</note> do this, for the year belongs to them. If the **Heavens** do this, then learn to recognize through this what the **Heavens** likewise work within us. We are so much stronger than the year, for we can turn the "weather" away from us; we can pick out what is good for us and cast away the evil. For we have within us the eternal **Summer**, which is never again without fruit and flowers; and it is the **Summer** that is to come, when no more years will be counted, but all years will be as a single moment.

<margin>Followers of true wisdom <note>original: "Veræ sapientiæ sectatores"</note></margin>
Therefore, in these things we should place our **Wisdom**, so that we are **Men** who do not arise from the **Heavens**, but from **Wisdom**. If we forget **Wisdom**, then we are beastly <note>original: "Viehisch"; living by instinct rather than reason or divine alignment</note> and live like a reed in the water, never safe for a single moment from a downpour that might come and carry us away. This is not to be feared in **Wisdom**, for she stands upon a rock, firmly built. Therefore look to your **Princes** and **Kings**, and those who rule there, so that you do not live merely "in the year" <note>meaning to live at the mercy of temporal cycles and luck</note>. The year gives roses, lilies <note>original: "Gilgen"</note>, and other flowers; it also gives beautiful pears in the field, and many good grapes; and silently along with these, the **Winter** creeps in, and all joy turns into a sorrow. # CONCERNING THE FRENCH <note>original: "DE GALLIS"</note>

If you do not let the **Heavens** <note>In this context, the author refers to living according to astrological fate or worldly fortune rather than spiritual "Wisdom."</note> rule, something further regarding them will happen in the future, as I will explain; for truly, what the **Heavens** elevate, they also let fall again. Therefore, read through the <term>Constellation: the relative positions of the stars believed to influence earthly events</term>, all you **Princes**, so that you may see how they stand over your dominions and so uncertainly, such that everything that dwells with and around you is your enemy. And just like the **rich man**, when he no longer has his money, he also no longer has favor and friendship; and just as money sustains him, so are you sustained by the **Heavens**. And just as money can dissolve for the rich man any day, so too can the **Heavens** renounce you any day; for in these things <margin>''</margin> it is a sandy building, with nothing to build upon. He who <margin>''</margin> puts his hope in money should measure how near he is to **God**, and just as near is he who puts his hope in the **Heavens**.

He posits concerning the **Palatine Princes** <note>The rulers of the Electoral Palatinate, a major territory of the Holy Roman Empire.</note> that they are good and mild, which is not to be contradicted. For if all **Princes** and **Lords**, all **Rulers** and **Cities** and **Lands** are considered in their generosity, these **Princes** exceed the common number. But see now that this goodness is no protection in an evil <term>Constellation</term>; rather, goodness and mildness fall into a heap along with other things. The cause of this is that they come from the **Heavens**, and the other things are also from the **Heavens**. And if the **Heavens** were not their patron, but rather **Wisdom** <note>The author distinguishes between the fleeting luck of the stars and the eternal stability of Divine Wisdom.</note>, then not a single drop <margin>''</margin> of their <term>Regiment: a term for a system of rule, governance, or administrative control</term> could be taken away. <margin>''</margin>

...ably. Now their strength has been taken from them, just as with the 
<margin>Sun-followers <note>original: "Sonnisten"</note></margin> ♌ [Leo] the Sun; from this their brightness was taken, and from that it follows that only those who are subject to the ☉ [Sun] will lose their brilliance.

Thus, know here of the **Palatine Princes** <note>The Electors Palatine, high-ranking rulers in the Holy Roman Empire, whose coat of arms featured a lion, linking them to the zodiac sign Leo.</note>, who belong entirely to ♌ [Leo] and the ☉ [Sun], and are completely devoted as children [to these celestial bodies]. So, when ♌ [Leo] stands well in the ☉ [Sun], then their realm also stands well, and they with the realm: but if some fortune falls from them, because they are its children, then it also falls from them. Thus has ♌ [Leo] in the ☉ [Sun] been driven into the world's assembly through the darkness, and has lost its cry along with the sunshine: and as every **eclipse** <term>Eclipsatio: a solar or lunar eclipse, historically interpreted as a disruption of the natural and political order</term> indicates a long, future adversity for the earth, air, water, and all growing things, so also know that in this bloodline such things also occur.

Therefore, know also, you princes, that you are greatly inclined toward the heavens and greatly compelled by them; so you should also know that there is no permanence in them, but rather what they give, they also take away again. Upon this you should guide and prepare yourselves, so that in your youth you do not let the heavens be your schoolmaster; for they persuade and command you according to their own right. But you ask no further than to consult the heavens for counsel, whether what you undertake is right or not: so know in this that the heavens give you weak counsel, just as if one blind person asked another to lead them for help.

### CONCERNING THE FRENCH <note>original: "DE GALLIS"</note>

...consider and measure within yourselves the great wisdom of the heavens, which is accomplished through you and your councils; but because these all come from the stars, it all falls to the ground. Therefore, do not hope or build upon your own cleverness and enterprise, for whatever does not come from God into you, that goes to ruin once more. Thus it happened to the **Lion** <note>Leo, the astrological sign and heraldic symbol of the Palatinate.</note> who placed his hope in the Sun, and honored the Sun as his God; and because it was an idolatrous worship, his fall occurred. If no support or foundation <note>original: "stutzlin oder grublin"</note> had been built and trusted in [human or astral] wisdom, then such a fall would never have happened to these princes.

But when the darkness <note>The solar eclipse previously mentioned.</note> began, then the enemies of the **Lion** were strong, and he was so weak that he hid himself; and the **Eagle** <note>The symbol of the Holy Roman Emperor, often in conflict with the Palatine Lions.</note> and all who were his followers, all of these had power and majesty over him. Thus, you princes: when your **celestial influence** <term>Influentz: the flowing down of ethereal power from the stars believed to dictate earthly events</term> is darkened, then everything that is with and around you becomes your enemy; and it happens so miraculously that it is thought impossible to fall into such enmity and misfortune.

Just as quickly as the Sun loses its radiance before the darkness, so too does misfortune overcome you; as **Liechtenberger** <note>Johannes Lichtenberger (c. 1426–1503), a famous court astrologer whose *Prognosticatio* was a definitive text for 15th-century political prophecy.</note> indicated, it has been fulfilled and has come to pass. What else the heavens have indicated for these princes in the future follows hereafter in the year 1440 and a few years more, after the **eclipse** <term>Eclipsatio: an eclipse, interpreted as a disruption of the natural and political order.</term>

# PROGNOSTICS

### On Chapter XXIV.

## Here sits a crowned lion, etc.

In this chapter, that which is **princely** is recounted; it befits a prince well to know—that is, to have so much **wisdom** <term>Weißheyt: here implying spiritual and practical discernment that transcends natural fate</term> within him—that he presses down beneath himself all heavenly power. For what is it that a star should drive away a prince? It is nothing but a sign of the prince’s great **folly**, who cannot withstand a star and does not have enough knowledge to prevent it; how then will he withstand the multitude of his enemies? Therefore, it befits a prince that he does not fear the **stars**, but is like a stone that lies in the sun, over which snow and rain fall—storm, lightning-strike, hail, etc.—yet the stone remains whole. Likewise, you princes and rulers, such a provident **spirit** <term>gemütth: the internal disposition, soul, or mind</term> should be yours, that you rule the heavens, and the heavens do not rule you. and break in: how these will be, however, is understood in the general interpretation of the **constellations**, regarding which things and uprisings the new inventions <note>The author uses "new inventions" or "new finds" (newen fündt) to mean political innovations or social upheavals</note> shall be the cause. For as often as a new invention, a new order, or a new **regime** <term>Regiment: a system of rule, government, or social order</term> enters, just as often a new **ascendant** <term>Ascendens: the specific zodiac sign rising at the start of an event, which was believed to imprint its character on that event</term> occurs, under which many new things begin. Blessed wanders he who leads his pilgrimage in **simplicity**; for this reason, he overloads himself with no star, and thus the **constellations** lie dead before him. But he who wishes to act with worldly cleverness and seeks to contemplate new advantages throws himself under the multitude of the heavenly stars, from which then follows a hard and unmerciful downfall. Consider also: if you place many "useful" **ascendants** in your chambers, before they are of use to you alone and not to others, they are merely of the heavens and have no stability. # ECLIPSE OF THE SUN
## EXPLANATION <note>original: "EXPLICATIO"</note>.

### Interpretation of the Solar Eclipse:

<margin>The Astronomer</margin>
So it should be, that everyone who wishes to write history begins after things have happened: for just as an unhappened thing may only be described with uncertainty by the writer of history, so also is it uncertain for the astronomer to describe that which it is his duty to write. Since the astronomer is now nothing other than a historian of the history of the heavens—whose histories are subsequently carried out on Earth through human beings—the historiographers <term>Historiographi: scholars who write history based on human records and events</term> differ from him in this: that they write the history of humanity, while the astronomers write the history of the heavens.

# EXPLANATION <note>original: "EXPLICATIO"</note>

<margin>The Historian <note>original: "Historiographus"</note></margin>
Therefore, it is fitting that the historian <term>Historiographus: a scholar who records history; here, Paracelsus argues they must understand celestial patterns to truly understand human ones</term> also be an expert in the history of the Heavens, for the upper and lower histories should be compared with one another. To describe the deeds of Man alone is not a complete labor, for every work becomes whole only when it stands as its own entity, such that nothing may be separated from it. For just as the carpenter cannot build or erect a house unless he first conceives a mental image of it—and from such a pre-formation the house arises—so too may he who understands that same mental image be a describer of the house that is destined to grow out of that plan.

<margin>Proclaimer of Men, Heaven <note>original: "Hominū pręco, Cœlū"</note></margin>
The deeds of Man have such a pre-formation <note>original: "vorbildung"; a blueprint or archetypal plan</note> in Heaven, specifically arising from its influence. What the Craftsman <note>original: "Wirckman"; referring to the divine architect or the creative force of nature</note> conceives in his head, the Heavens also conceive. For the Heavens are the single "head" of all men, wherein those things are ordered which Man conducts through earthly reason, wisdom, cleverness, foresight, and so on. Therefore, it is only right that such a description of "un-happened" histories <note>referring to events that have been planned in the stars but not yet enacted on Earth</note> be written first from this "head" of mankind, followed thereafter by the foundation of the histories of men. Who would be displeased by this, or not consider it a beautiful history, to set the Heavens as the primary subject and the human being afterward? How pleasant and lovely such a description would sound! Thus, I cannot omit here to describe a little something, the Nobility...

# EXPLANATION. 145

...the aforementioned process <note>original: "melter Proces"</note> includes much more that ought to be known, which perhaps has remained unlearned through negligence.

<margin>Proposition</margin>
Now, to proceed with the preface I have begun—in order to describe the history of the heavens and to prepare the way for the historians <note>original: "Historiographis"</note>—I have taken up the eclipse of the sun that recently occurred in Regensburg <note>A major Imperial city in Bavaria</note>. I intend to describe its significance there, having attempted to do so some time ago but being prevented by adversity. Thus time and seasons pass away; yet who gives the astronomer room for his description while the very hour of the event is passing? Now the work on the eclipse must be undertaken and delayed no longer. It shall be a complete judgment, for it is written after the heavens have passed and after the deeds have actually occurred, just as it is proper to describe history from its very foundation.

I wish to admonish all those in the field of astronomy that they should not judge my writings too hastily. Nor should they be hostile toward them through the power of the <term>Art of Points <note>original: "Artis punctorum"</note>: A reference to Geomancy, a method of divination using random patterns of dots or marks</term>, which many practitioners especially attempt to champion. With what sort of "artful" manner they do this is well known. Let each person be granted permission and authority within his own faculty; they should be allowed to instruct their own peers in what their artful methods are capable of revealing.

<margin>Geomancy</margin>

<header>SOLAR ECLIPSE <note>original: "ECLIPSIS SOLIS"</note></header>

## Beginning and explanation of the Practices.

It is well known to you all that our wisdom, with which we act upon the Earth, lies within us no differently than bread that has not yet been leavened <note>The author uses the archaic term "gehäffelt," referring to the process of adding yeast or leaven to make dough rise</note> and prepared by sourdough. Such is our wisdom without the influence of the stars: for each person’s wisdom is fermented by the heavens. From this it follows that from many kinds of ferment, many kinds of senses and thoughts arise, <margin>,,</margin> each time being different from the other.

<margin>,,</margin> Thus, if our reason must have such a fermenting agent, <margin>,,</margin> and that through the heavens, then the sun is the only ferment of the heavens. It is the sun itself that leavens the wisdom we use on Earth, whether it be in craftsmanship, the arts, counsel, and so on. For all things occur and are guided by wisdom. Therefore, mark this: the sun is the ferment and the leaven of our wisdom; and according to how the sun prepares us, so are we then "baked."

Now, on the other hand, you should know that there are many kinds of people in their various offices <note>In this context, "office" refers to one's professional role, calling, or social station</note> upon the Earth. One fulfills his office with mildness, another with cunning and wars, another with faithlessness and envy, unchastity, deception, lying, and so it goes on. But however that may be, no one can carry out these things, as has been indicated, without...

<header>ECLIPSE OF THE SUN <note>original: "ECLIPSIS SOLIS"</note></header>

...[foundations] I speak of this: that he who is of the morning of the future end, etc. is known [to be one who] treats the foundation of <term>Astronomy <note>original: "Astron."; likely referring to the study of the stars' influence on human life.</note></term> out of the heaven, or shows the eighth heaven from his birth—he also belongs to the heaven. ### PRACTICE.

<margin>The Hunger-cloth of the Sun. <note>The "Hungertuch" (Lenten veil) was a cloth used to hide the altar during Lent; here, the author uses it as a metaphor for the obscuration of the Sun during an eclipse.</note></margin>
Now further, just as there is nothing among perishable things that is without adversity, so also the Sun <note>represented by the symbol ☉</note> is covered with a "hunger-cloth," as has been described above in whatever way by those slanderous astronomers; it is not necessary to stain my book with it here. Regarding this hunger-cloth, which has occurred and has covered the Sun's image, brilliance, shine, etc., it is a great thing to consider: namely, that the Wisdom of the Ancients, which existed before this darkness, will come to an end. And just as the Sun descends in the sign of Cancer <note>original: "Krebs"</note>, so too will their wisdom descend; for they rose with the Sun, which has now fallen into darkness, as one says in natural reason: A thing that reaches its highest point falls down again, just like a tree: when it is at its highest, it rots and dies. It is no less important to consider that such a rising must descend, as impossible as this...

## Preface of Doctor Bombast <note>The family name of Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), the famous physician, alchemist, and occultist.</note> /
# ON THE FOUR-YEAR PRACTICE. <note>original Latin: "IN PRACTICAM QVATVOR ANNORVM."</note>
### Four-Year Practice.

<margin>practice.</margin>
Setting aside all weather-forecasts <note>original: "Wetterpracticen"</note> and those things which they comprise that have appeared this year, there follows an account of events <note>original: "Geschichten"</note> which will occur this year, which are unknown to those same [astrologers]. For their work is produced not with perfect skill, but with defects. The reason for this is that a different order and process must be followed; for the entire subject matter belonging to a Prognostication <note>A formal prediction of future events based on astronomical or spiritual signs.</note> (which they call a "Practice"), while it is not merely one art, it is also not merely one work. Even if not in matter alone, it is divided so that each science <note>original: "scientia"</note> should be pursued until its very end, with nothing left out. This is the error that practices have held until now. Although I omit rain, thunder, snow, and wind—because others satisfy their desires by attending to them—I leave those things to them. They do not know how to climb higher, but delight in small things, merely painting over the work of the Ancients, as well as including the New and Full [Moons], etc., and whatever else is patched together. Whoever desires to know such things will find them with those others. Dearth <note>original: "Teure"; likely referring to *Teuerung*, a period of high prices or scarcity of food.</note>

<summary>In this preface, Paracelsus critiques contemporary astrologers for focusing on superficial weather predictions and copying ancient methods. He argues that a true "Practice" or prophecy requires a comprehensive scientific approach to understanding the events of the coming years.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, Prognostication, Practice, Scientia, Weather-forecasting, Astrology, Historical Events, Alchemical Philosophy</keywords>

<margin>False practitioners <note>original Latin: "Pseudopracticis"</note></margin> the more important points should be addressed/ those very ones which cannot lead to harm/ but rather teach how to prevent harm/ being especially serviceable/ useful to an entire **Community**/ Rich and Poor/ High and Low/ which are not dependent on the purse/ but on necessity. For it is so precarious now to investigate the influence of the heavens/ that one should show it to no one/ keeping everything hidden/ especially from the **Authorities**/ let alone that it should reach the **Common people**/ and be presented to so many thoughtless heads and arrogant interpreters.

Although I also fear here/ that this **Prognostication** of mine will also fall into the hands of such incompetent readers/ so it will be the last time/ that they shall learn from me: I wish/ that all **Astronomers** were of this mind/ and

# PRACTICA <note>A "Practica" was a common type of annual astrological almanac or practical guide during the Renaissance, offering predictions for weather, politics, and health.</note>

<margin>Proposition.</margin>
In the past year, a **Prognostication** <note>An astrological forecast of future events.</note> was issued by me, the title of which stood for four years; however, it was made too difficult and too short for some, which did not happen without cause: for
<margin>Children of heaven.</margin>
the **children of heaven** <note>Paracelsus uses this term to describe people who are overly dependent on astrological influences or who believe their fate is entirely dictated by the stars.</note> are so sharply focused on the **influence of heaven** <note>The "influx" or "influence" refers to the invisible forces believed to flow from the stars into the human body and mind.</note>, that they direct all their doing and leaving [actions] according to it—whether to curse, to kiss, to stop, or to become quarrelsome. For truly, where the heaven leaves them, they are already despaired of God, because for them, the heaven in their heart is their God. Therefore they read every **Practica**, which are their teachings and their hearts; when they do not find what they understand, they grumble. Their belly, their God, [their] need, demands it, so that they might more easily come upon a further explanation of that same **Practica**. Although it would be more honorable for them to read and speak of the time from God, they have something else in mind. It is a precarious thing to show the course of the heaven in signs and works now, for every man wants to have his praise; and if the heaven shames him, he still wishes to be praised. Who can make the mild **Jupiter** out of the grim **Saturn**, or the refined **Venus** out of the unrefined **Mars**? <note>Paracelsus refers to the traditional astrological temperaments: Saturn is cold/melancholy, Jupiter is benevolent, Mars is aggressive, and Venus is harmonious. He argues that the fundamental nature of these cosmic forces cannot be changed by human whining.</note> Now, who can follow so many mad heads, according to each one’s own craving? They judge and want to force, not the way [of God], but that the way must go out [their own way]; they want to bluster at their Father the Heaven, **Saturn**, **Mars**, who do not let themselves be blustered at, except through God, from those same [to take...]

<summary>Paracelsus defends a previous multi-year astrological forecast and critiques "children of heaven" who treat the stars as their God to justify their own selfish desires and temperaments. He emphasizes that astrological influences like Saturn or Mars cannot be bullied or changed to suit human vanity.</summary>

### OF FOUR YEARS <note>original: "QVATVOR ANNORVM"</note>

X iij

...they bring together their harmful **wisdom**. The lights <note>Referring to the celestial bodies, such as the sun, moon, and stars.</note> are so diverse, and there is so much **salt** <note>In Paracelsian thought, "salt" is one of the three prime substances (Tria Prima) representing materiality and stability. Here it may imply a clutter of physical details that distract from spiritual truth.</note>, that their own **planets** take displeasure in this; therefore they move toward the end and wish to strike their children with the final rod; yet the **Last Day** <note>The Day of Judgment or the end of the world.</note> is not yet here, though good blows are. Although the **over-rational** ones <note>original: "Vbervernünfftigen"; those who rely too heavily on human intellect rather than spiritual insight.</note> calculate and announce the hour of the **Last Day**, there is rarely a heart that is without a fool; should the heavens then not have their own jester <note>original: "gauch"; literally a cuckoo, used here to mean a fool or someone who speaks nonsense.</note>, who speaks more than is commanded? This is what the **highly wise** do: they go to plow in the **Platonic Year** <note>original: "anno Platonis"; a "Great Year" or cosmic cycle of approximately 25,800 years when the stars return to their starting positions.</note> and in the number seven; it looks almost like your foolery (if it were fitting to speak thus of the highly wise). But one must be lenient with those who walk not in simplicity, but in **wisdom**.

<margin>“ “ “</margin>
Now, if such great reason is to be used out of Nature—whether it be from a wise Nature or the Nature of fools—every **astronomer** <note>original: "Astronomo"</note> must know: first, that he should not rely on the heaven's influence alone, but also consider the **prophecy** <note>original: "weissagung"</note> which is not from the heaven (that is, from **Christ** <note>original: "Christo"</note>). For if Christ did not testify and point to the signs as a warning to us, who among us believers would believe the heaven? No one. But because Christ points us there, to learn and recognize His word of **prophecy** from that very source, it is honorable to exhort every believer to take heed...

<summary>Paracelsus critiques those who attempt to calculate the exact time of the Last Judgment using astrology, arguing that true prophecy must be rooted in Christ rather than just the movement of the stars. He suggests that while nature and "the lights" provide signs, they must be interpreted through faith and divine warning rather than mere intellectual cleverness.</summary>

to present that same passage <note>original: "locum"; referring here to a specific location in the heavens or a point in a text.</note> in the stars <note>original: "astro"</note> concerning this time. Regarding this, I remind you that you understand these things in God, attributing them to Heaven. So, if you now return to the origin of the first prophecy <note>original: "Weissagung"</note>, you now know who is being announced, or who **Mercury** <note>The text uses the astrological symbol ☿ for the planet Mercury, which often represents messengers, commerce, or intellect.</note> is, whom Christ means. As all things are, take the first sign for yourselves: that the heaven is "hard." This refers to love for one's neighbor; for whoever does not have it, the signs are directed against them. The second sign is greater than this, but because it is internal while the other is outwardly manifest, the manifest remains here, and the internal remains within each individual.

4. The concluding speech of the same.

# Interpretation of the First Prognostication
## of the four Years.
### Chapter I. <note>original: "Caput I."</note>

Special meanings are intended for those who are against Heaven, even though they do not want to belong to Heaven. The reason is that they do not wish to acknowledge their true Father, instead naming themselves after another father, whose **bastards** <note>original: "Spurij"; from the Latin *spurius*, meaning illegitimate or base-born children.</note> they are. Therefore, pay attention only to the first sign that is mentioned; within it, everything shall be concluded.

**Point 1: Rebellious Lords, etc.** <note>original: "1 Punctus: Auffrhurische Herrn"</note>

The two Lords have not been named, nor is it proper to name them. One has torn a kingdom out of Europe, and this encroachment shall go further, though not as quickly as some have supposed. This year, he will be called a "Heart" <note>original: "Hertz"; this may be a wordplay on the title "Herzog" (Duke) or a symbolic reference to a central figure.</note> and was born to a heart.

**II. Guests of Europe, etc.**

Europe has its head in **Germany** <note>original: "Germania"</note>; for Germany is the head of Europe. In the head of Europe there are

->III. Two Sheep, etc.<-

The sheep will not care a whit for any of the shepherds; therefore, the sheep will graze themselves and will despise the shepherds, from which contempt a great resentment will grow, directed toward all shepherds, both the righteous and the unrighteous.

->IV. Exchanging Justice, etc.<-

That is, some are robbed of what they had, and others are given liberties and the like, which happened more in the year just past and will happen even more in the current year. <note>original: "Justitiam" (Latin). not my cities, but the cities whose will is not represented in the sign <note>original: "zeichen"; likely referring to an astrological omen or a symbolic figure in a woodcut.</note>.

### VII. Great Adultery.

Like nuns and monks, who are adulterers <note>The author likely considers the breaking of monastic vows to marry—a common occurrence during the Reformation—as a form of spiritual or legal "adultery."</note>, they shall be separated; that is the adultery, for each one is permitted his vice just as he began it in his heart, though he speaks otherwise with his mouth.

### VIII. Several Sects shall arise.

It is a union formed so that their kingdom does not appear destroyed, where one yields to the other; the private intentions of the aforementioned will dissolve, and other sects will cast themselves off (more so than being cast off by others) and "hang their cloaks" <note>original: "den mantel hencken"; a German idiom meaning to be an opportunist or to turn one's coat to match the prevailing wind.</note> so that the frost <note>original: "reiff"</note> does not fall into the chest. This is also to be understood regarding the rebellious sects: they entered with rebellion <note>original: "auffrhur"</note>, and because they will not remain steadfast in their first purpose, they will hide like reeds, withdraw into revocation <note>original: "Reuocation"; a formal recanting or withdrawal of previous religious statements.</note>, and step backward. But the constellation <note>The alignment of stars and planets used to predict earthly events.</note> shows them no support in many matters; therefore, they will cause more rebellion, which will ultimately strike themselves.

### IX. Nine Substantially Learned Lights, etc.

Many interpretations have been made regarding this, specifically assuming them to be "lights of the sects," yet I cannot call them "lights" <note>The author may be making a pun here between "Liechter" (lights) and "leichter" (easier or lighter/frivolous), expressing skepticism about the wisdom of these leaders.</note>, as astronomy instructs me.

### X. The IX Sects will sacrifice, etc.

### XII. To be raised in faith, etc.

After their shepherds sneak away—those from whom the very thing that makes them shepherds is taken.

### XIII. In this time there is patience, etc.

# Interpretation of the Figures that the man from Nuremberg found in the Carthusian Cloister, which pertains to this present year.

## Chapter III. <note>original Latin: "Caput III"</note>

There was (according to the claim) found in Nuremberg in the Carthusian Cloister an old painting with many strange figures; now, they are not failing, for they hit the mark <note>original German: "treffen den rechtschuldigen"—literally "hit the one who is rightfully guilty," meaning the prophecy accurately identifies its target</note>. But the crowd of the inexperienced is so hasty; they have come forward with a gloss <term>Gloss: a commentary or marginal note used to explain a difficult text</term> and interpreted them with a double meaning, provided with a preface and such things. Although that same interpreter despised these things, vanity <note>original: "Narz"—likely a reference to Narcissus, symbolizing self-love or pride</note> has nonetheless stung him, as if he were carrying it for another; in the end, if one were to ask, no one would want to be responsible for it. Now, it must be someone, even if it were strongly denied. He is the first "apostle" to interpret the images, and perhaps announces—or fears—that it suits his Diana <note>A reference to the Roman goddess; here likely used as a metaphor for a specific idol, obsession, or a "great lady" in a coded political sense</note>.

## The second part concerning other occurring points of this year.

<margin>Astronomy and Medicine are parts of Theology <note>original Latin: "Astronomia & Medicina particulæ Theologiæ"</note></margin>
The greatest part of this Prognostication <term>Prognostication: a forecast of future events based on the study of celestial signs and symbols</term> is to be treated from the foundation of Astronomy, so that everyone may know and recognize what is being said and upon what basis. For if the foundation of Astronomy is not known, then enemies may use their terrors and turn the reader away—namely those enemies who use Theology with new tricks <note>original: *schwäncken*, referring to deceptive arguments or clever schemes used by critics</note>. Now, Astronomy is a part of Theology, for Christ directs us toward the knowledge of the signs which Christ displays. He is not a theologian whom Astronomy does not move <note>original: *rürt*, meaning to touch or affect</note>; the Word of Christ is complete. Likewise, Medicine is also a part of Theology, for Christ directs the sick to them; thus, Medicine confirms the wisdom of Christ. For it is not enough to only read what Christ has spoken, but rather to fulfill the same—that is true Theology, etc.

<margin>The true aim of Theology <note>original Latin: "Verus Theologiæ scopus"</note></margin>
It is clear in the Word of God: "Love your neighbor." Who can do this? Only the complete theologian can do it. Is not making the sick healthy one [of the acts] that belongs to this commandment? Now, the signs proclaim only one thing by showing and presenting [it]; but those who, through ambition, haughtiness, etc., pride, wealth, [strive] after—

<margin>The Book of Revelation <note>original: *Apocalypsis*</note></margin> 
What is the **Book of Revelation** but a form of *Astronomy*? For it treats solely of these signs which *Christ* mentioned and warns us when the time comes. What else is it, being described like *Revelation* and being **Holy Scripture**, yet having no other foundation than the dream, the voice without a body, and *Visions* <note>original: *Visiones*</note>? Who can reject these things? The heaven does not give them to itself, but to us: for we cannot give ourselves any sign; it is from the heaven that one receives them, and He is the **Creator** of us all. Therefore, the signs should truly serve as an interpretation of the **Word of Christ**, which must be performed only by the *Astronomer* of the "first Astronomy." 

<margin>Magic <note>original: *Magia*</note></margin> 
**Magic** is not called "weather-forecasting"; rather, through it, the four arts of the four "stars" <term>stars: here referring to the four elements or spheres of the world</term> are found to be righteous: **Geomancy** <note>divination by earth</note>, **Pyromancy** <note>divination by fire</note>, **Chaomancy** <note>divination by the air or atmosphere</note>, and **Hydromancy** <note>divination by water</note>. ...[recognize] it, and not within himself, nor that such things are **auguries** <note>original: *Auguria*; the ancient practice of interpreting omens from the flight or cries of birds</note> or **superstition** <note>original: *Aberglauben*</note>; they are the workings of heaven, yet hidden from those who have no foundation in their own **faculty** <note>original: *facultet*; here referring to their professional field or natural ability</note>.
