<margin>The Raven <note>original: *Corvus*</note></margin>
That the **raven** <note>original: *Rabe*</note> announces the **death-rattle** <note>original: *Todkräcken*</note> is not its own nature, but rather its **ascendant** <note>original: *Ascendens*; the astrological sign rising on the eastern horizon, believed here to act as an internal driving force</note> which is within it; it smells the **corpse** <note>original: *Cadauer*</note> like a certain carrion.

Thus all these things come from heaven, that is, from the **stars** <note>original: *Gestirn*</note>. But the **one governed by Mercury** <note>represented by the symbol ☿; in this context, it refers to a person of a specific intellectual or perhaps deceptive temperament</note> is a **hypocrite** <note>original: *Hipocrita*</note> who shames heaven; he attributes these things to the **Devil**, as if the Devil had made heaven and its stars, and not God. If you refuse to recognize the wonder-works of God, then you only know how to chatter, and you reveal who you truly are.
<margin>Hypocrite <note>original: *Hipocrita*</note></margin>

Just as the stars teach each creature to proclaim according to its own **kind** <note>original: *specie*</note>, so it shall be proclaimed without any deception or human addition; and by watching closely—as it is written at the end of the **Revelation** <note>original: *Apocalypsi*; referring to the biblical warning in Revelation 22:18-19 against altering the prophecy</note>—one should neither add nor take anything away from it. The reason is this: man did not devise it, but heaven has given it for the **interpretation** <note>original: *außlegūg*</note> of God’s Word, from which we are all born. Since heaven, and not man, has given this, then man has nothing to add to or take away from heaven, which...

# PRACTICA

Heaven remains free in its essence / for nothing upholds it / but the hand of God / those who would add to it / improve it, or the like / outside of the **royal prerogatives of the art** <note>original: *kunst Regalien*; refers to the fundamental laws or "sovereign rights" governing the study of the stars, established by God</note> / they falsify their own writing / for in Heaven alone it stands / not in Man. And because Man does not understand Heaven / what its secret is / so it also remains unpunished by us / who know nothing / but what is **muddy** <note>original: *kotticht*; derived from *Kot* (mud or filth), implying that human knowledge without divine insight is base or earthly</note>. Thus is the power of this **prognostication** <note>original: *Prognostication*; a foretelling of future events based on signs</note> in these things / whoever wishes to have the right **interpretation** <note>original: *Auf-leger*; literally "one who lays out" or explains</note> of these signs / let him go into the **Gospel** <note>original: *Euangelium*</note> / there it will be explained: the signs are those / which follow hereafter concerning this year, regarding good and evil / whereupon each person should learn to recognize the people whom they signify / and seek the interpretation in the place / where Christ spoke of it.

Now the signs are briefly to be recognized in this / that all things / which are encountered on Earth according to **Christ’s prophecy** <note>original: *Christi weissagung*</note> [occur] first in Heaven: from which it then follows that no **historian** <note>original: *Geschichtschreiber*</note> may write the deeds of men / unless he has knowledge of the upper Heaven / which further indicates these and other deeds and teaches how to write them. For:
» our names are written above in heaven /
» that is / our work / **actions and omissions** <note>original: *thun vnd lassen*; a common legal and moral idiom for everything a person does or fails to do</note> / take place in the upper Heaven / there God sees / what we do on Earth / from that same operation follows thereafter / what Man does.

Therefore, power and authority are granted to the **astronomer** <note>original: *astronomo*; here meaning one who studies the stars to discern divine will rather than modern material science</note> to reveal the lower world through the upper, insofar as Heaven instructs and teaches him. ### The outcome of the signs / Sun / Moon / Darkness / having passed in the thirtieth year.

First, there is the proof that an **eclipse** <note>original: *Eclipsis*</note> is a sign, which Christ presents in the moon and stars; note it thus: As far as the sunshine reaches, that area is named "the Sun"; whatever occurs there is "within the Sun." The same applies to the moon; now, whatever occurs there must also be recognized as originating from the same Heaven in which the sun and moon reside. From that same source comes into the sun and moon that which Christ has spoken of.

Now darkness enters into this—which God could certainly have directed in another way—but it must proceed as a **prophecy** <note>original: *Weissagung*</note> and a confirmation of His Word.

### Doctor Paracelsus’s Preface to the Reader.

There is a proverb among the learned: A wise man overcomes the stars <note>This refers to the famous Latin maxim *Sapiens dominabitur astris*, suggesting that while stars influence, reason and will can prevail</note>; yet at this

<margin>,,</margin> weighs, and considers everything, man does exactly 
what the heavens want, and even more. Where 
the heavens instill a tiny grain <term>quintlin; a very small unit of measure, roughly equivalent to a dram or about 4 grams</term>, man makes 
ten pounds out of it, and is thus even worse 
than what the heavens indicate or impress upon him. 
In these recent years, the planets have never appeared 
so poisonous <note>In 16th-century astrology, "poisonous" refers to malefic or negative planetary influences that were thought to corrupt human health or social order</note> as things have transpired on Earth; 
but man is so inclined that even if a tiny spark 
falls into him, he reaches much further than that spark 
indicates or leads. For although Mars <note>In astrology, the planet Mars is associated with conflict, war, anger, and heat</note> 
kindles but a little, man carries it on without measure,

# PRACTICE.

## The Third Chapter, on how each planet will conduct itself toward those who are inclined toward it.

<margin>«</margin>
<margin>«</margin>
As it has been a custom from old tradition until now, that the planets have always been characterized in relation to their subjects: But now, a distinction shall henceforth be made, not according to the person, but according to their <term>occupation <note>original: "handthierung" — refers to one's trade, craft, or social function. The author argues that in this era, a person's professional role is a more significant astrological marker than their individual character.</note></term>; for now at this time, the occupation governs the man, and the man the occupation; according to what they are, they shall be understood and recognized.

### First, concerning Jovian occupations.

Jupiter is divided and torn within himself in his occupation, and those who are <term>Jovian <note>original: "Jouisch" — those influenced by Jupiter, traditionally associated with the clergy, high officials, and judges.</note></term> occupy his office: but not the <term>Saturnians <note>original: "Saturnij" — those influenced by Saturn, often associated with laborers, monks, or the elderly.</note></term>, the <term>Martials <note>original: "Martiales" — those influenced by Mars, such as soldiers or smiths.</note></term>, or the <term>Venerians <note>original: "Veneristen" — those influenced by Venus, such as artists or lovers.</note></term>. Therefore, his kingdom shall have no rest, unless those who were born of him return to his <term>inclination <note>original: "Inclination" — the astrological "leaning" or natural tendency a planet imparts to a human soul.</note></term>; however, this cannot happen this year. Therefore, the division will remain, and none among them shall prosper, but the Jovians shall become estranged from others.

<summary>This page discusses a shift in astrological interpretation, moving from personal character to professional "occupation" as the primary influence of the planets. It specifically predicts internal strife and division within the Jovian class (officials and clergy) for the year 1537.</summary>

THE YEAR 1537

## Concerning those of Mercury.

Those who possess the nature of Mercury shall and must be born of Mercury; for no one becomes an artist unless they are born to it through a special inclination from Mercury. For this reason, Mercury is rarely deceived by his children—that is, he does not take in <term>adoptees <note>original: "Adoptiuos" — refers to those who "adopt" a trade or craft without having the natural, planetary talent for it.</note></term>. Therefore, if they are rightly born of him, they shall have good success this year. However, where (as is to be feared) the two eclipses of the Moon <note>original: "zwo finsternuß des Mons" — likely a variation of "Monds." The author previously noted two lunar eclipses occurring in 1537 that would bring ill fortune.</note> affect them, then every sick person should be on guard, as should every poet, astronomer, gold- or silversmith, painter, stonemason, sculptor, and whoever deals with books and exerts themselves in making amusing jests, or deals in such matters; for spiteful talk shall increase, along with minor sorrows.

## Concerning those of Saturn.

The occupations that are classified under Saturn: such as masons, monks, furriers, carpenters, tanners, shoemakers, <term>miners <note>original: "Ertzknappen" — literally "ore squires," a respectful historical term for those who worked in the mines.</note></term>, saddlers, weavers, tailors, etc. Although all these might not be Saturnine in their personal nature, their occupation is nonetheless Saturnine, and by it they are governed, so that they properly—

### Concerning those of Venus.

**That** the frivolity of people toward unchastity and the like (which are then called womanly or amorous natures) occurs, should not be attributed to **Venus** <note>original: "Veneri" — the Latin dative form of Venus.</note>, but rather to innate and nurtured frivolity. Incited and instructed in such things—as they are all called—their judgment for this year is that they will lead many astray and deceive one another. Yet, they will find goodwill before the **authorities**, just as they do before **God**, as may well be judged. It has now come to the course of the **planets**, and yet more is attributed to them than they are actually guilty of.

## Concerning the Mercurials.

Those who possess the nature of Mercury <note>original: "Mercurij" — the Latin genitive form of Mercury.</note> should and must be born of **Mercury**; for no one becomes an artist unless he is born to it through a particular inclination from Mercury. Therefore, Mercury is seldom deceived by his children—that is, he does not accept any <note>original: "Adoptivos" — adopted children, meaning those without a natural talent or "calling."</note>. For this reason, if they are truly born of him, they shall have good success this year. However, where (as is to be feared) the two eclipses of the Moon <note>original: "Mons" — an archaic spelling of "Monds" (Moon).</note> affect them, let every sick person beware, as well as every **Poet**, **Astronomer**, **Goldsmith**, **Silversmith**, **Painter**, **Stonemason**, **Sculptor**, and those who deal in **books** and apply themselves to cracking amusing jokes or deal in such matters; for boastful talk <note>original: "Weidsprüch" — likely referring to hunting tall-tales or exaggerated speech.</note> will increase, accompanied by little trust.

<header>OF THE YEAR 1537</header>

## Concerning those of a Martial Nature.

Those who are subject to <term>Mars <note>original: "Marti" — the Latin dative case. Mars was the god of war, and in astrology, he governed conflict, fire, and metalwork.</note></term> will not easily conquer this year, nor will they be profitable. For others will intervene; in their trades and occupations, they will be overshadowed by the <term>eclipses <note>original: "Finsternuß" — literally "darkness."</note></term> of the first and second <term>Moons <note>original: "Mons" — an archaic spelling of "Monds." This refers to lunar eclipses.</note></term> in May and November, which will overtake them with the three aforementioned points. However, those who are armorers, weaponsmiths, knifesmiths, farriers, and locksmiths will have a good and profitable year; but the <term>alchemists <note>Practitioners who sought to transform base metals into gold; their work was associated with Mars because of the heavy use of furnaces and fire.</note></term> should expect no progress.

## Concerning the Children of the Sun.

Those whom the <term>Sun</term> rules will become like servants, in that they will allow themselves to be led by the eclipses; this is spoken of individual persons such as Kings, Princes, and Lords. Furthermore, those who govern in the manner of councils, territories, or cities will go very far astray and hold many consultations that will be without fruit <note>meaning the meetings will be unproductive or fail to yield results.</note>. And although this year not only territorial or city councils would go astray, but other councils of even greater importance would take place, they would be corrupted because of the two kinds of lunar eclipses and mixed with those same articles, so that more evil than good will arise from them.

<summary>This page predicts a difficult year for soldiers and rulers due to lunar eclipses in May and November 1537, while noting that smiths and craftsmen will remain prosperous.</summary>

## The fourth Chapter concerning the Divisions
## that now currently appear.

Regarding the divisions <note>original: "Spaltungen"—referring to the religious and political schisms of the Reformation era.</note>, many discussions will take place and be well-concluded, but poorly executed; they will decrease, none will increase, and they will turn around and change significantly. However, as for uniting themselves, nothing will be found this year, nor will people meet one another peacefully, except for what must occur through cunning, fear, or difficulty; yet the heart will agree with wherever the mouth leads <note>A cynical observation that people will pay lip service to peace while their true intentions follow their public posturing.</note>.

## The fifth Chapter concerning Peace 
## and Strife.

Germany will be without war within itself. Also Hungary, Dalmatia, <term>Croatia <note>original: "Krabaten"—an archaic German term for the Croats.</note></term>, Poland, Prussia, Bohemia, and Denmark will show themselves to be somewhat quarrelsome, but nothing will be dangerous. France, England, <term>Guelders <note>original: "Geldern"—a historical duchy in the Low Countries, now part of the Netherlands and Germany.</note></term>, and <term>Stettin <note>A major port city in Pomerania.</note></term> will howl for peace, worry, and wait. Italy will get through this year with good, gentle winds; Milan will be endured. <term>Savoy <note>original: "Sophoy"—the Duchy of Savoy.</note></term> will turn green again, and whatever is joined to France will lean toward the beginning of destruction.

A powerful military expedition <note>original: "Hörzug"—likely a variation of "Heerzug," referring to an army on the march.</note> will be damaged and struck quite hard, through which much blood will be shed, but it will not be brought to a full conclusion.

### The sixth Chapter, concerning the 
### fertility of this year.

Furthermore, in this year, a fruitful season will appear, sufficient and quite productive; although it will be somewhat hindered by rain and frost, the damage will be compensated for in another location. Likewise, the wine will turn out well, as will many other garden crops, although some thunder is to be feared, though not over a wide area.

### The seventh Chapter, concerning the 
### illnesses of this year.

The diseases that are coming this year will primarily be those that prevail in common lands as foretold by the eclipse <note>original: "finsternuß"—referring to an astronomical event interpreted by the author as a medical omen.</note>, and in several places, pestilence, side-stitches <term>side-stitches <note>original: "stich in Seyten"—an archaic term often referring to pleurisy or acute respiratory pain.</note></term>, chest ailments, and the like. It will be worrisome for pregnant women who are of a "wrathful" nature <note>original: "zorniger natur"—referring to the Galenic theory of the Four Humors, specifically the Choleric temperament associated with yellow bile and heat.</note> and for young children, as well as those of a weak constitution <term>constitution <note>original: "Complexion"—in early medicine, this referred to the specific balance of humors within an individual's body.</note></term>.

PRACTICA <note>This title, Latin for "Practice," refers to a practical guide or almanac, a very popular genre in the 16th century used to predict weather and events based on the stars.</note> <insert>ANNA</insert>

### The eighth Chapter, concerning the new and Full Moons, the quarters before and after, along with their condition and nature.

#### The New Moon of February. <note>original: "Hornung"—an archaic German name for February, possibly referring to the time when stags shed their antlers.</note>

The New Moon of February will occur on the eleventh day of January at five o'clock in the afternoon and twenty-one minutes; it will be fine, windy and pleasant, dry, but cold.

The first quarter will be on the day of St. Anthony <note>January 17th. In this era, people tracked the days of the month by the feast days of Saints rather than just numbers.</note> also dry and cold.

#### The Full Moon of February.

The Full Moon is on the day after the Conversion of Saint Paul <note>The feast of the Conversion of Saint Paul is January 25th; therefore, this refers to January 26th.</note>, at six o'clock before noon, inclined toward unstable weather.

The last quarter, on the second day of February, is inclined toward more inclement weather <term>inclement weather <note>original: "vngeschickligkeyt"—literally "unsuitability" or "clumsiness," but in a meteorological context, it refers to harsh or unfavorable conditions.</note></term>.

#### The New Moon of March.

The New Moon of March will be on the day of Saint Scholastica <note>February 10th.</note> at eighteen minutes past three o'clock before noon; Cold, dry, and windy.

Its next quarter will be on the day of Saint Juliana <note>February 16th.</note>, fresh, healthy, and clear, with little instability.

### Full Moon of March.

The Full Moon will occur on the day following the feast of Saint Matthias <note>February 25th. Matthias was the apostle chosen to replace Judas Iscariot.</note> at eleven o'clock and eleven minutes in the morning; unsettled and inclined toward much wind, along with unsettled snow and rain.

The New Moon of April will occur at twelve o'clock noon and thirty-six minutes on the day before the feast of Saint Gregory <note>March 11th. In this era, "New April" refers to the lunation that will govern the month of April.</note>; it will be windy and unsettled.

The Full Moon will also be unsettled on the day following the Annunciation of Mary <note>The feast of the Annunciation is March 25th; the forecast refers to March 26th.</note>.

Its quarter on the second day of April will be fair and dry.

The New Moon of May will be at nine o'clock and fifty minutes in the evening on the day after the feast of Saint Mary of Egypt <note>Saint Mary of Egypt's day is April 2nd; the forecast refers to April 3rd.</note>; fair, clear, and calm.

Its quarter will be on the sixteenth day of April, also like the New Moon.

# THE ALMANAC OF THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS,

<margin>(1530.)</margin>
Prepared for the year after Christ’s birth 1539; in honor of the most powerful prince and lord, Emperor CHARLES <note>referring to Holy Roman Emperor Charles V.</note>, concerning the coming time of this year; and as an instruction to all astronomers <note>term for both astronomers and astrologers in this period.</note>, which until now has never
->been maintained.<-

This year, Mercury and Jupiter, with the help of Venus, will support the heavens in the firmament, but in their spheres <note>original: "Firmamenten," referring to the planetary shells in the geocentric model.</note> they shall not rule over earthly matters. Also, past influences—some ending this year and others beginning—under Saturn and Mars, are entrusted to the time to come.

### PRACTICAL WORK OF THEOPHRASTUS <note>original: "PRACTICA THEO.", referring to "Practica Theophrasti," the practical application of Paracelsus's astrological and medical theories.</note>

<margin>The superstition of astrologers <note>original Latin: "Astrologoru̅ superstitio."</note></margin>
<margin>(1521)</margin>
presently the planets threaten: For every influence <term>influence (influenz)</term> <note>In the 16th century, "influence" was viewed as a literal "flowing in" of celestial power that affected human health and destiny.</note> of the planets to which humanity gives space, to that same influence the end is also given. Herein know that, up to this point, a remarkably large number of heavenly influences have been accepted and permitted by humans, whereupon it is now fitting to bring those same ones to a conclusion.

Since these same influences have been accepted by humans in such a manner that they are driven toward their end, it follows that while this persists and is not yet completed, no [new] planetary influence should be further investigated. For because the old influences are not yet finished, the new influences cannot carry out their effects or their will. On this account, I am moved to give instruction to all astronomers <note>A term then used for both astronomers and astrologers.</note> who take up this practice: look more closely at the accepted influences of the past and declare them truly. For as long as those [past influences] are working within the human mind <note>original: "gemütth," referring to the soul or inner disposition.</note>, during that time no new influence can take hold.

<margin>Jupiter ♃ Mercury ☿</margin>
Therefore, because Jupiter is seen to be powerful this year, as is Mercury, there would be much to practice <note>to forecast or calculate</note> concerning them according to ancient tradition. But because the old effect is not yet extinguished, I may in no way this year—

<meta>catchword: be- (likely beginning the word "beschreiben" [describe] or "berichten" [report] on the following page)</meta>

<summary>Paracelsus argues that astrological forecasting is often inaccurate because practitioners ignore "old" celestial influences that are still playing out in the human mind. He suggests that new planetary alignments cannot take effect until the momentum of previous astrological cycles has been fully exhausted.</summary>

# OF THE YEAR [15]39.

...describe the meaning and the future; therefore, I do not wish to describe their <note>Referring to the planets or celestial signs mentioned on the previous page.</note> properties in detail at this place, as it would be in vain to offer human indications concerning what is involved there. For this reason, I take up for myself what has remained from the Ancients <note>The "Alten" refers to classical authorities in astrology and medicine, such as Ptolemy.</note> and is not yet completed, but rather will be brought to an end this year. I shall discover whether something might happen this year that you could compare to the [influence] springing from the two planets; you should not measure these [planets] by their current positions, but rather as a past influence that is now preparing for its conclusion.

<margin>The burning intention of men <note>original Latin: "Hominũ ardens intẽtio."</note></margin>
Many people have generally pleaded with all astronomers to apply themselves in this way: to look upon the burning passion of the mind <term>mind (Gemütth)</term> <note>A term in Paracelsian philosophy referring to the seat of human soul, will, and emotion.</note> that humans adopt. Consider that this same passion, with its heated purpose, does not end easily, but persists slowly according to its [initial] influence. It imprints itself so highly and strongly upon the human heart that no planet can cause a rupture or introduce its own nature into such a mind. Therefore, it would be improper to announce a foreign <note>In this context, "foreign" means a new or different astrological effect that contradicts the current state of the person's will.</note> effect while the old purpose is still active and its end is yet to be fully expected. It is also impossible for any new impression <term>impression (Impression)</term> <note>The astrological belief that celestial bodies "stamp" or "imprint" their qualities onto the human soul like a seal on wax.</note> to move such a "kindled" heart toward other things.

<page-num>204</page-num>
<header>PRACTICA THEO. <note>likely an abbreviation for *Practica Theoretica*, a common title for works combining practical astrological prediction with theoretical explanation</note></header>

...therefore, I now speak freely for this year regarding all those things that might concern the nourishment of the body. If the **stars** <term>Astra: the celestial bodies and their perceived influence</term> had power in such matters, I would observe their course more closely; but if a **dearth** <term>Theurung: a period of scarcity, high prices, or famine, usually regarding grain or wine</term> should occur, it is not to be attributed to the **constellations**, but solely to those who observe more in them than is actually there to be seen.

<margin>The fault of men, not of the stars. <note>original: "Hominũ culpa, non siderum."</note></margin>A scarcity will occur, but it will be uneven; this is caused only by the fact that the **planets** are not opposed to us, but rather that we assume much and attribute it to the planets, whereas the planet bears neither the guilt nor the cause. In the same way that the **heavens** are credited with governing the **earth**, even more is attributed to their **reason** <term>Vernunfft: here referring to a perceived guiding intellect or logic within the celestial spheres</term>; that is to say, the heavens stand for themselves, and the earth likewise, through which such occurrences may well be recognized.

The heavens bring no scarcity this year, but rather a fruitful and blessed year to those **regions of the earth** which are destined for fertility. But to those from whom the **mother** <note>original: "gebärerin," referring here to the earth as the "bearer" or producer of crops</note> has withheld her gifts, you should not attribute this to the heavens; for what **Nature** gives in her harvest happens without hindrance from the other **element** <term>Element: in this context, the author refers to the celestial "element" or sphere as distinct from the earthly one</term>.

A scarcity will occur not from the heavens, but from **mankind**; therefore, we cannot judge the heavens nor their nature, but must look more to the nature of the other three **elements** <note>The terrestrial elements: Earth, Water, and Air</note> which cry out against the heavens.

<page-num>205</page-num>
<header>IOHANNI XXXIX. <note>likely a reference to a specific chapter or section within the larger work</note></header>
<sig>DD iij</sig>

...mother earth.

The **scarcity** <note>original: "Theurung," referring to periods of high prices and food shortages</note> will not be the fault of the crops, but rather of those who have authority over them. In short, no scarcity will be inflicted upon the common people this year, though many troubles related to scarcity will arise that the heavens will not cause. These issues will be stirred up by those who predict the most profound future events for the following year.

Much hail and thunder will occur here and there, as well as other things that may harm the earth. None of these things are entirely subject to the **planets**, even though nature has its origin <note>literally "birth"</note> there and hides its matter within them; yet the planets have no command over the outcome. Therefore, I leave these matters aside for now, and I do not blame the planets this year for the desolation and ruin of the earth. I state generally that this year no one who makes their living from the land will have cause to complain; for the earth will prevail to such an extent that many will lose faith in [the influence of] the heavens <note>The author suggests that a good harvest will prove the astrological "experts" wrong, causing people to doubt celestial omens</note>.

For although some frost will affect the fruits of the earth, and there will be many incidents of thunder and hail, it remains impossible for an **astronomer** <term>Astronomus: a scholar who studies the stars to predict both physical weather and human fate</term> to explain the specific purpose or "command" of these storms. Although every year in the **practical almanac** <term>Practick: a popular type of annual calendar providing astrological forecasts for weather, crops, and health</term> mention is made of profit and loss, we should not attribute these things to the heavens, but rather to those who venture to extract more from the heavens than is actually in them:

<meta>catchword: Therefore (original: "Darumb")</meta>

But although hidden things will be discovered this year, they should all be attributed to the upper **firmaments** <term>firmamenten: in the cosmology of the time, the spheres of the stars that were thought to influence the physical world</term>; therefore, let every man love that which is commanded of him in such a way that he maintains peace with his neighbors, according to what his desire indicates. And if the earth complies with them in this way, there will be more good fortune than had been expected, even if frost, snow, or hail are added. Therefore, let everyone be content with what the earth grants him, and let the poison be taken by another.

## Concerning the Scholars this Year.

Scholars will not particularly bother one another this year, but will remain quiet; yet they will drift away <note>original: "abschiffen," literally 'to sail away,' here meaning to deviate or set a different course</note> in their minds and thoughts, not acting harshly toward one another. They will also prize their own freedom so highly that some among them will forget themselves. Many among the scholars will abandon their old intentions this year, which they themselves will come to despise; however, a new idea will occur, at which the elders will be somewhat terrified.

Regarding the discipline of scholars, many will fall away from their masters; this, however, will result in injury to both sides and become the origin of an opposing faction.

Those who occupy themselves with languages <note>referring to the Humanist focus on the "three sacred languages": Greek, Hebrew, and Latin</note> will not be held in high regard this year; they will be significantly hindered in their intentions in the following year, and those who were educated by them, or their like, will be turned away in the year to come.

<page-num>212</page-num>
<header>PRACTICAL THEOLOGY.</header>

### Concerning the Nobility.

The Nobility will have good peace this year as far as the course of the heavens <note>original: "Himmels lauffs," referring to the movement of the planets and stars in astrology</note> is concerned. However, what they inflict upon themselves will not be insignificant. They will, in part, divide themselves into factions and provide the very cause for the breaking of their own power. This year will give them cause to reclaim much that had been withdrawn—property or rights that had been cast out of their rule for several years. This will be for them like "recovered goods," <note>original: "gewonnen gutt," likely referring to assets or territories reclaimed after a period of loss</note> the greater part of which will be completed in the following years.

### Concerning Merchants.

Those merchants who travel will not trade with profit; they drive even their friends away from their goods. As for the general trade of merchandise, the heavens will not be subservient <note>meaning the stars will not be particularly favorable or offer a guaranteed path to success</note>; instead, success will be left to the individual Reason <note>original: "Vernunfft," a recurring theme in this text suggesting that personal judgment is more powerful than fate</note> of each man.

### Concerning Craftsmen.

This year brings no change for them; they will remain in their daily undertakings, but in their services, they will allow for much yielding <note>likely meaning they will have to make concessions in their prices or labor conditions</note>.

Observe: In this year, many things will be set in motion <note>original: "anzetlē," literally to "warp" a loom, meaning to begin a process or hatch a plan</note>, but nothing will be finished. Some things will be extinguished, and some will be saved for the following years.

### Concerning Mortality This Year.

No great mortality <note>original: "Sterben," which in this period specifically referred to a plague outbreak or high death rate</note> will particularly arise this year. Although various illnesses will be discovered, the influence of this year will be nothing more than to reveal all illnesses. These illnesses will appear with strange and wondrous signs <note>original: "Warzeichen," meaning symptoms or diagnostic portents</note>, such that the likes have not often been heard of among doctors. Because of this, some might think the planets are exerting a special influence this year, but this is not to be considered at all. Rather, many will be driven to death and illness this year for reasons that the planets neither provide nor take away. In some places, pestilence and sores will be found, but not generally; instead, they are leftovers from other times. No new illness will arise on account of the planets this year. Note also that many natural illnesses arise of their own accord, which some astronomers might attribute to the planets; this, however, occurs out of the simplicity <note>meaning a lack of depth or professional ignorance</note> of those practitioners. <margin>Caution <note>original: "Cautela"</note></margin>

# TO THE ASTRONOMERS [BY] DR. BOM-
# BAST: FROM THE NORTH COM-
# ES ALL EVIL. <note>original Latin: "AD ASTRONOMOS D. BOMBAST: AB AQVILONE VENIT OMNE MALVM"</note>

->From the North comes all evil.<- <note>original German: "Von Mitternacht kompt alles böses." In this period, "midnight" was commonly used to denote the direction of the North.</note>

<margin>A comparison of the stars with the elements. <note>original Latin: "Astrorum cū Elementis collatio"</note></margin>
<image-desc>Large ornamental drop cap 'A' in a square woodcut border featuring floral and foliate patterns.</image-desc>
Aquilo <note>the North wind or the Northern region</note> is the region where this comet rises, which moves into the Caurus <note>original: "Coles"; the northwest wind</note> wind, [then] Africus <note>the southwest wind</note>, which is a companion of Auster <note>the south wind</note>, and then back again into the North, because it was born and manufactured in the North. And from the North come all evils; for that reason it is a heavy <note>original: "tenert," possibly meaning dense, dark, or somber</note> thing. Not that its beauty, or that it gathers its beauty or itself from the vapors of the Earth—as some followers of philosophy <note>specifically referring to the Aristotelian view that comets were atmospheric vapors</note> maintain—for no star stands in the firmament that takes its birth, body, and light from the lower elements. The Heavens have their elements, the lower realms have theirs, and each has its own regional characteristics. But so that its origin may be revealed, it is like the earthly authorities <term>authorities: "Obrickeyt," referring to the governing magistrates or ruling legal powers</term>. Just as you see that the vices and excesses of men cause the earthly authorities to impose punishment upon them—whether with the rope, sword, rod, etc.—and then we are punished by the same [authority] because such guilt has been committed against the authority. Thus know, that men [provoke] the Supe- <note>The text cuts off here and likely continues to the word "Superior" or "Supreme Authority" on the following page</note>

<summary>Paracelsus addresses his fellow astronomers, arguing that comets are not mere atmospheric vapors but celestial entities born in the North to serve as divine warnings. He compares the appearance of a comet to earthly magistrates who only impose punishment because human vice necessitates it.</summary>

# FROM THE NORTH etc. <note>original Latin: "AB AQUILONE &c."</note>

## LETTER TO A FRIEND <note>original Latin: "EPISTOLA AD AMICVM"</note>

Greetings. <note>original Latin: "S. D." abbreviated for "Salutem Dicit," a standard formal greeting meaning "sends health/greetings"</note> Although neither my generosity nor my services were ever found at your command, distinguished Doctor, yet since your **physical state**, **condition**, and **temperament** <note>original Latin: "Physica & conditio & complexio"; refers to the person's constitutional health and balance of bodily humors</note> are very evident and known to me, I have taken the boldness to write, believing myself to be most welcome to you. Therefore, I commit this brief **little work** <note>original Latin: "Opusculum"</note> on the approaching **comet** to your examination, as one placed among the coals of your scrutiny; our illustrious Lords of **Zurich** <note>original Latin: "Turrecensibus," referring to the people or city of Zurich</note> made you known to me. I also pray you, who are most capable with even a single word, to take care that this **interpretation** is printed immediately. Do not let the title or the printing of the rest cause you concern as you weigh the beginnings. I hope you are fortunate with these—entirely fortunate—for if it were the contrary, I would certainly have appeared to you. Other matters I have confided privately. For I do not wish my letters to be shipwrecked in the sea of your business. Thus be content with these very brief words, and farewell, O sincere one among learned men, by the stars of God, and farewell. M. given [at the hour]

TH. H.S. L. Professor of both **Medicines** <note>original Latin: "vtriusq; Medicinæ," likely referring to both the theoretical and practical branches of medicine</note>, Doctor of Arts, and propagator of **Philosophy**.

### FRAGMENTA <note>fragments</note>

...comes forth and strokes <note>meaning to flatter or smear</note>, then Satan is there with his cunning and leads you to school—into the school of eternal damnation.

<margin>The Writing of History <note>original Latin: *Historiographia*</note></margin>
* It is fitting that one should undertake to describe the "day of history"—that which has happened—since the astronomer <note>in this era, astronomy and astrology were a single discipline focused on interpreting the heavens</note> is the initiator and interpreter of the description of history. For who would want to describe the history of man and yet forget astronomy? Therefore, the highest description is to describe the heavens, and afterwards man, for man is the one who follows after in walking before the heavens and their stars.

<margin>Human precursors <note>original Latin: *Hominũ precursores*</note></margin>
Man is high and is well-guarded, placed and formed in the image of God; it is fitting that he should have a precursor and a guide <note>original German: *Vorwandler*, one who walks before to prepare a path</note>. God the Son had His precursor, John the Baptist, etc.

<margin>Let judgment be free <note>original Latin: *Iudiciũ sit liberum*</note></margin>
* Let me speak on this, you astronomers, and be not so angry with me that I separate myself from you in my writing, solely for the sake of the Light <term>Light of Nature: a philosophical concept referring to the innate wisdom or divine intuition present in the natural world</term>, as far as nature is to grasp it. Do not heed my following; heed Him whom you do not see.

<margin>Dreams <note>original Latin: *Somnia*</note></margin>
One thing is necessary for you to consider (though you are hostile toward it): the dream. What is the dream? Nothing but preludes of things, monstrous shapes <note>original Latin: *rerum præludia, figuræ monstrósæ*</note>, etc. What should I present to you, or of whom should I remind you, if not of the creatures? From whom did man come, if not from Him who went before him? Shall go—

<summary>The author argues that history and human existence cannot be understood without astronomy, as the heavens serve as a precursor to human events. He also introduces the significance of dreams as symbolic "preludes" to reality, urging astronomers to look beyond the visible world.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s defense of his prophetic method. On the previous page, he argued that man requires a celestial guide and that astronomers must follow the "Light of Nature." ### OF PROGNOSTICS,

...let it have its place here; let the history be as it is; you and I shall discuss it. We have two paths: you wish to keep to your own, in which you will fail; I walk mine. Therefore, what I write here is nothing else than to precede the **writing of history** <note>original Latin: *historiographia*</note>, for I wish to describe that which they intend to describe only after the deeds have already occurred. You will resent me for this, but I shall proceed and disregard your objections. 

What is to happen there—the most necessary thing—is to be described from the Heavens. It <note>the Heavens/the stars</note> has brought these things about; from it they are described: reason, understanding, fortune, victory. What else? Nothing. Therefore, there are so many who do not recognize the Heavens and only report that things have happened as they did; [they do not see] that the Heavens describe great **historians** <note>original Latin/Greek: *Historiographos*</note>—that is, the description of the Heavens itself. 

It is of timely importance to me to describe the present matters, which I completed at Regensburg <note>an important Imperial city in Bavaria where the author likely finished this work</note>. Time tends to consume itself; but He who gives space and place to every time and moment, He arranges it all, for He holds both in His hand. Thus, it is now time to describe the **eclipse** <note>original Latin/Greek: *Eclipsin*</note> that has occurred. I would have much preferred the time that preceded it, but then that which has now occurred would not have happened. 
<margin>"</margin> An unfulfilled Heaven cannot be described historically; 
<margin>"</margin> for to describe histories, there must be histories in the Heavens just as there are among men. 
<margin>"</margin> Otherwise, what is the use of writing? It is nothing but a dream, what...

Now, the wisdom of Nature alone is the highest understanding of things born of God. For he is wise who carries out his office wisely; that office is not only in **government** <term>government: *Regiment* in the original, referring to the administration of social or political order</term>, but also in **craftsmanship** <term>craftsmanship: *Handtwerck*, encompassing all forms of skilled trades and manual labor</term>, for both are wisdom, both are equal gifts, both are called craftsmanship. Hereupon it depends on us how we wish to use them—one for this, the other for that—spiritual and worldly government, or labor; wherever one applies it, there he will be occupied according to what the degree of the Sun gives him. Now, for 300 years, the time has not stood in the sharpness of wisdom; however, because the other planets must take their wisdom from the Sun and can carry it, and man takes the same from them: upon this follows in man a great opening, secret wisdom and prudence in all things upon which he sets his mind. Now, as it is so, you see that the Sun casts high those who desire wisdom and gives them their desire, for desir-

# OF PROGNOSTICS <note>original Latin: *PROGNOSTICORVM*</note>

...comes from the root, which corresponds to the fruit. G.* for *Heiliger Geist* in the original, suggesting a spiritual or clerical calling</term>, and so forth with all the actions that are present in Man.

Concerning these things, it should also be known that the power of the Sun has exceptionally taken hold in many matters; therefore, in brief, know the conclusion: whatever has come from the Sun—through those influenced by Saturn, through those influenced by Venus, and through those influenced by the Sun itself—shall fall to the ground. One part is to be counted in the first year from this **eclipse** <note>original: *Finsternuß*, meaning darkness or an eclipse, often viewed as a prophetic omen of decline</note>, one part in the half-year; thereafter the other two parts in the year following that; and in the fifth year, all the others shall perish. For in this way, the Sun begins to descend on **St. Vitus’ Day** <term>St. Vitus’ Day: June 15th (*S. Viti*), which in the old calendar was the approximate date of the Summer Solstice, after which the days begin to shorten</term> toward sorrow; it withers away until it is parched. Likewise, those whom the Sun has exalted and raised up shall wither away, just as the Sun also raises the dry tree upon the earth so that it blooms and turns green, and then leaves it again—that is, for this time, the half part is over, and now it turns toward Winter.

Now, although the other six planets also have a share and fellowship in such matters, and not only the Sun, yet that which excels, in that very thing is the

# FRAGMENTS <note>original Latin: *FRAGMENTA*</note>

Sun / the sharpness of the Sun / the Sun illuminates exceedingly. But whatever travels within it, travels safely; for it is so ordered that its wisdom <note>original: *klugheyt*</note> attaches itself to things, or creates an attachment to itself, and it still demonstrates the effect of that same attachment for the benefit of the whole group. Therefore, the Sun speaks to war, to peace, to unity, and to unrest, according to how its wisdom is distributed, just as it has done in many ways throughout times past.

Now, in that place, there will be no ceasing nor overlooking; whatever attachment, counsel, etc., has triumphed through the Wisdom of the Sun will go toward its decline in this or that matter. The peaceful Sun will grow; the unpeaceful will be stilled; and unrest will be soundly beaten. 

<margin>World-warriors</margin> Look closely now, you **world-warriors**; it concerns you who have exceeded others. If they have poured out their wisdom entirely, it will happen that they must maintain it themselves without further help. The Sun looks upon its servants; your heart has become darkened, its court has been rejected and disbanded; let each man help himself as best he can. 

It is not that I write out of envy; it is true that the Sun has highly **exalted** <term>Exalted: In astrology, a planet is "exalted" when it is in the specific zodiac sign where its influence is most powerful and beneficial</term>. <header>FRAGMENTS</header>

...happen often; therefore, it remains in common usage. Whoever wishes to interpret these signs for himself—those which are currently increasing—must take for himself the great **Conjunction** <note>the alignment of major planets, particularly Jupiter and Saturn, believed by Renaissance thinkers to signal major historical shifts</note> and nothing else outside of this **Doctrine**. The common course of events indicates nothing at this hour and time regarding what is presently at hand: Although they are and remain signs, as Christ has spoken, the prophecy of the **Gospel** <note>original Latin: *Euangelij*</note> is not to be understood in that way, but rather as the daily course of events. *

<margin>Planetary work.</margin>
This year, certain **Practices** <note>original: *Practiken*; these were popular astrological calendars or prognostications sold to the public</note> have been published; do not let them lead you astray; it is the nonsense <note>original: *tådung*; literally "dulling," referring here to the empty or deluded chatter of "old wives"</note> of old women and planetary work. It is not necessary for the common man to know the course of **Mars**; they manage well enough in these matters without it. Likewise, regarding weather, rain, etc., and also what they otherwise say from their hearts this year; they dream that things will "bite more sharply" <note>an idiom suggesting that conditions will become more severe or difficult</note>; they will have to experience it differently, etc.

Much is said: this will happen, that will happen, etc.—a dying <note>referring to a period of high mortality or plague</note>, war, famine, etc. It happens; from whence do we know it? From the face of the heavens; but we do not know what will come of it. For example: people spoke of the Great Flood; from where was such a **Prophecy** <note>original: *Weissagung*</note> taken? From the face of the heavens. It is just as when someone has jaundice <note>original: *Geelsucht*</note> and one says, "You will die, I can see it on you"—it happens or it doesn't; who knows how he is on the inside? Likewise the **Plague** <note>original Latin: *Pestis*</note>, etc.; these are <margin>To judge by the face <note>original Latin: *Facietenus iudicare*</note></margin> judgments spoken from the "face" and not from the heart, where life lies. <summary>This page critiques the popular astrological "Practices" of the time, arguing that predicting war or plague from the "face of heaven" is like diagnosing a patient's death based solely on their outward appearance rather than their inner condition.</summary>

<page-num>235</page-num>
### ON PROGNOSTICATIONS <note>original Latin: *Prognosticorum*</note>

"The Last Day is near"—what is that, other than they judge the sky by its face, yet they lie in the interpretation? For no one knows this, not even the angels in heaven; and the scribes <note>original: *Schriftgelårte*; refers here to the learned theologians or legal scholars</note> forget the Word of God and do not consider that, since it is not known to the angels, it is much less known to men. For since we are meant to keep watch, and not know when he comes, etc., what do the celestial signs concern us? The Last Day will not stand out in the face of the sky <note>original Latin: *in facie coeli*</note>, but rather will come like a thief, etc. Therefore, what can one say about it through art <note>referring to the "art" of astrology or divination</note>, since Christ rejects that art and gives the parable of the thief? For this reason, such writers are rightly called worthless <note>original Latin: *Nequam*; a term for a wicked or good-for-nothing person often used in Latin translations of the Bible, such as the "wicked servant" in Matthew 25</note> by Christ. What then should we say of the heavens? Nothing except the morning red, the evening, etc. <note>a reference to Matthew 16:2–3, where Jesus tells the Pharisees they can interpret the weather from the color of the sky but not the "signs of the times"</note> So much power <note>the ability to observe or interpret</note> we have, and no more. And we should not even predict the rain; one will experience it when it arrives. For predicting such things causes great offense, because they are in God’s hand; He turns them as He pleases. If it does not occur, what comes of it but mockery? If it does happen, what have you gained? You were still not certain of the thing. *O immortal God, who gives the rain from your providence and faithful care, etc.* <note>original Latin: *O Deus immortalis, qui das pluuiam de tua prouidentia & fideli cura, &c.*</note>

---

->THE END.<- <note>original Latin: *FINIS*</note>

<summary>The author concludes by condemning astrological predictions of the end times, arguing that the Last Day will come unexpectedly "like a thief" and that humans should only observe the weather, leaving the future to God's providence.</summary>
